\displaystyle\trace\big[(\boldsymbol{I}_{T}\otimes\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{\hat{{S}},\hat{{S}}})(\boldsymbol{I}_{T}\otimes(\boldsymbol{X}_{\hat{{S}}}^{\top}\boldsymbol{X}_{\hat{{S}}})^{\dagger})\big]\lVert\boldsymbol{F}\rVert_{\rm{F}}^{2}

\displaystyle=~

\displaystyle T\trace\big[\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{\hat{{S}},\hat{{S}}}(\boldsymbol{X}_{\hat{{S}}}^{\top}\boldsymbol{X}_{\hat{{S}}})^{\dagger}\big]\lVert\boldsymbol{F}\rVert_{\rm{F}}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq~

\displaystyle T\trace\big[(n\eta)^{-1}\boldsymbol{X}_{\hat{{S}}}^{\top}\boldsymbol{X}_{\hat{{S}}}(\boldsymbol{X}_{\hat{{S}}}^{\top}\boldsymbol{X}_{\hat{{S}}})^{\dagger}\big]\lVert\boldsymbol{F}\rVert_{\rm{F}}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq~

\displaystyle\frac{T}{\eta}\left\lVert\boldsymbol{F}\right\rVert_{\rm{F}}^{2},

where the penultimate inequality uses
\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{\hat{{S}},\hat{{S}}}\preceq(n\eta)^{-1}\boldsymbol{X}_{\hat{{S}}}^{\top}\boldsymbol{X}_{\hat{{S}}}
in the event
U_{1}\cap U_{2}
.
Therefore, on
U_{1}\cap U_{2}
, we have

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n}\sum_{ij}\left\lVert\frac{\partial\boldsymbol{F}/D}{\partial z_{ij}}\right\rVert_{\rm{F}}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq 4\max(1,(\eta)^{-1}(T\wedge\frac{p}{n}))+2n^{-1}[4\max(1,(2\tau^{\prime})^{-1})]^{2}

\displaystyle:=f(\eta,T,n,p),

where the function
f
is the same as in case (1). The only difference is that
\tau^{\prime}
in the upper bound for case (1) is replaced by
\eta
in case (2).

\blacksquare

H.3
Proofs of results in Appendix
E

The following proof of
Lemma

E.1
relies on a similar argument as proof of
Lemma

D.4
, we present the proof here for completeness.

Proof of
Lemma

E.1
.

Recall the KKT condtions for
{\widehat{\boldsymbol{B}}}
defined in (
6
):

1)

For
k\in\hat{{S}}
, i.e.,
{\widehat{\boldsymbol{B}}}{}^{\top}\boldsymbol{e}_{k}\neq\mathbf{0}
,

\boldsymbol{e}_{k}^{\top}\boldsymbol{X}^{\top}\big[\boldsymbol{E}-\boldsymbol{X}({\widehat{\boldsymbol{B}}}-\boldsymbol{B}^{*})\big]-n\tau\boldsymbol{e}_{k}^{\top}{\widehat{\boldsymbol{B}}}=\frac{n\lambda}{\|{\widehat{\boldsymbol{B}}}{}^{\top}\boldsymbol{e}_{k}\|_{2}}\boldsymbol{e}_{k}^{\top}{\widehat{\boldsymbol{B}}}\quad\in{\mathbb{R}}^{1\times T}.

2)

For
k\notin\hat{{S}}
, i.e.,
{\widehat{\boldsymbol{B}}}{}^{\top}\boldsymbol{e}_{k}=\mathbf{0}
,

\left\lVert\boldsymbol{e}_{k}^{\top}\boldsymbol{X}^{\top}\big[\boldsymbol{E}-\boldsymbol{X}({\widehat{\boldsymbol{B}}}-\boldsymbol{B}^{*})\big]-n\tau\boldsymbol{e}_{k}^{\top}{\widehat{\boldsymbol{B}}}\right\rVert<n\lambda.

Here the strict inequality is guaranteed by Proposition 2.3 of
[
4
]
.

Let
\ddot{\boldsymbol{B}}=\frac{\partial{\widehat{\boldsymbol{B}}}}{\partial E_{it^{\prime}}}
,
\dot{\boldsymbol{E}}=\frac{\partial\boldsymbol{E}}{\partial E_{it^{\prime}}}
. Differentiation of the above display for
k\in\hat{{S}}
w.r.t.
E_{it^{\prime}}
yields

\boldsymbol{e}_{k}^{\top}\boldsymbol{X}^{\top}(\dot{\boldsymbol{E}}-\boldsymbol{X}\ddot{\boldsymbol{B}})-n\tau\boldsymbol{e}_{k}^{\top}\ddot{\boldsymbol{B}}=n\boldsymbol{e}_{k}^{\top}\ddot{\boldsymbol{B}}\boldsymbol{H}^{(k)}

with
\boldsymbol{H}^{(k)}=\lambda\|{\widehat{\boldsymbol{B}}}{}^{\top}\boldsymbol{e}_{k}\|_{2}^{-1}\left(\boldsymbol{I}_{T}-{\widehat{\boldsymbol{B}}}{}^{\top}\boldsymbol{e}_{k}\boldsymbol{e}_{k}^{\top}{\widehat{\boldsymbol{B}}}~\|{\widehat{\boldsymbol{B}}}{}^{\top}\boldsymbol{e}_{k}\|_{2}^{-2}\right)\in{\mathbb{R}}^{T\times T}
. Rearranging
and using
\dot{\boldsymbol{E}}=\boldsymbol{e}_{i}\boldsymbol{e}_{t^{\prime}}^{\top}
,

\boldsymbol{e}_{k}^{\top}\boldsymbol{X}^{\top}\boldsymbol{e}_{i}\boldsymbol{e}_{t^{\prime}}^{\top}=\boldsymbol{e}_{k}^{\top}[n\ddot{\boldsymbol{B}}\boldsymbol{H}^{(k)}+(\boldsymbol{X}^{\top}\boldsymbol{X}+n\tau\boldsymbol{I}_{p\times p})\ddot{\boldsymbol{B}}].

Recall
\boldsymbol{P}_{\hat{{S}}}=\sum_{k\in\hat{{S}}}\boldsymbol{e}_{k}\boldsymbol{e}_{k}^{\top}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{p\times p}
. Multiplying by
\boldsymbol{e}_{k}
to the left and summing over
k\in\hat{{S}}
, we obtain

\boldsymbol{P}_{\hat{{S}}}\boldsymbol{X}^{\top}\boldsymbol{e}_{i}\boldsymbol{e}_{t^{\prime}}^{\top}=n\sum_{k\in\hat{{S}}}\boldsymbol{e}_{k}\boldsymbol{e}_{k}^{\top}\ddot{\boldsymbol{B}}\boldsymbol{H}^{(k)}+\boldsymbol{P}_{\hat{{S}}}(\boldsymbol{X}^{\top}\boldsymbol{X}+n\tau\boldsymbol{I}_{p\times p})\ddot{\boldsymbol{B}},

which reduces to the following by
\supp(\ddot{\boldsymbol{B}})\subseteq\hat{{S}}
and
\boldsymbol{X}\ddot{\boldsymbol{B}}=\boldsymbol{X}_{\hat{{S}}}\ddot{\boldsymbol{B}}
,

\displaystyle\boldsymbol{X}_{\hat{{S}}}^{\top}\boldsymbol{e}_{i}\boldsymbol{e}_{t^{\prime}}^{\top}

\displaystyle=n\sum_{k\in\hat{{S}}}\boldsymbol{e}_{k}\boldsymbol{e}_{k}^{\top}\ddot{\boldsymbol{B}}\boldsymbol{H}^{(k)}+\boldsymbol{X}_{\hat{{S}}}^{\top}\boldsymbol{X}_{\hat{{S}}}\ddot{\boldsymbol{B}}\boldsymbol{I}_{T}+n\tau\boldsymbol{P}_{\hat{{S}}}\ddot{\boldsymbol{B}}\boldsymbol{I}_{T}

\displaystyle=n\sum_{k\in\hat{{S}}}\boldsymbol{e}_{k}\boldsymbol{e}_{k}^{\top}\ddot{\boldsymbol{B}}\boldsymbol{H}^{(k)}+(\boldsymbol{X}_{\hat{{S}}}^{\top}\boldsymbol{X}_{\hat{{S}}}+n\tau\boldsymbol{P}_{\hat{{S}}})\ddot{\boldsymbol{B}}\boldsymbol{I}_{T}.

Vectorizing the above yields

\displaystyle(\boldsymbol{e}_{t^{\prime}}\otimes\boldsymbol{X}_{\hat{{S}}}^{\top})\vec(\boldsymbol{e}_{i})

\displaystyle=[n\sum_{k\in\hat{{S}}}(\boldsymbol{H}^{(k)}\otimes\boldsymbol{e}_{k}\boldsymbol{e}_{k}^{\top})+\boldsymbol{I}_{T}\otimes(\boldsymbol{X}_{\hat{{S}}}^{\top}\boldsymbol{X}_{\hat{{S}}}+n\tau\boldsymbol{P}_{\hat{{S}}})]\vec(\ddot{\boldsymbol{B}})

\displaystyle=(n{\tilde{\boldsymbol{H}}}+\boldsymbol{I}_{T}\otimes(\boldsymbol{X}_{\hat{{S}}}^{\top}\boldsymbol{X}_{\hat{{S}}}+n\tau\boldsymbol{P}_{\hat{{S}}}))\vec(\ddot{\boldsymbol{B}})

\displaystyle=\boldsymbol{M}\vec(\ddot{\boldsymbol{B}}).

A similar argument as in Proof of
Lemma

D.4
leads to

\displaystyle\vec(\ddot{\boldsymbol{B}})=\boldsymbol{M}^{\dagger}\boldsymbol{M}\vec(\ddot{\boldsymbol{B}})=\boldsymbol{M}^{\dagger}(\boldsymbol{e}_{t^{\prime}}\otimes\boldsymbol{X}_{\hat{{S}}}^{\top})\boldsymbol{e}_{i}.

Therefore, by
\boldsymbol{X}\ddot{\boldsymbol{B}}=\boldsymbol{X}_{\hat{{S}}}\ddot{\boldsymbol{B}}
,

\displaystyle\frac{\partial F_{lt}}{\partial E_{it^{\prime}}}

\displaystyle=\boldsymbol{e}_{l}^{\top}\frac{\partial\boldsymbol{E}-\boldsymbol{X}({\widehat{\boldsymbol{B}}}-\boldsymbol{B}^{*})}{\partial E_{it^{\prime}}}\boldsymbol{e}_{t}

\displaystyle=\boldsymbol{e}_{l}^{\top}\big(\boldsymbol{e}_{i}\boldsymbol{e}_{t^{\prime}}^{\top}-\boldsymbol{X}\ddot{\boldsymbol{B}}\big)\boldsymbol{e}_{t}

\displaystyle=\boldsymbol{e}_{l}^{\top}\boldsymbol{e}_{i}\boldsymbol{e}_{t^{\prime}}^{\top}\boldsymbol{e}_{t}-\boldsymbol{e}_{l}^{\top}\boldsymbol{X}\ddot{\boldsymbol{B}}\boldsymbol{e}_{t}

\displaystyle=\boldsymbol{e}_{l}^{\top}\boldsymbol{e}_{i}\boldsymbol{e}_{t^{\prime}}^{\top}\boldsymbol{e}_{t}-(\boldsymbol{e}_{t}^{\top}\otimes\boldsymbol{e}_{l}^{\top}\boldsymbol{X}_{\hat{{S}}})\vec(\ddot{\boldsymbol{B}})

\displaystyle=\boldsymbol{e}_{l}^{\top}\boldsymbol{e}_{i}\boldsymbol{e}_{t^{\prime}}^{\top}\boldsymbol{e}_{t}-(\boldsymbol{e}_{t}^{\top}\otimes\boldsymbol{e}_{l}^{\top}\boldsymbol{X}_{\hat{{S}}})\boldsymbol{M}^{\dagger}(\boldsymbol{e}_{t^{\prime}}\otimes\boldsymbol{X}_{\hat{{S}}}^{\top})\boldsymbol{e}_{i}

\displaystyle=\boldsymbol{e}_{l}^{\top}\boldsymbol{e}_{i}\boldsymbol{e}_{t^{\prime}}^{\top}\boldsymbol{e}_{t}-\boldsymbol{e}_{l}^{\top}(\boldsymbol{e}_{t}^{\top}\otimes\boldsymbol{X}_{\hat{{S}}})\boldsymbol{M}^{\dagger}(\boldsymbol{e}_{t^{\prime}}\otimes\boldsymbol{X}_{\hat{{S}}}^{\top})\boldsymbol{e}_{i}

\displaystyle=\boldsymbol{e}_{l}^{\top}\boldsymbol{e}_{i}\boldsymbol{e}_{t^{\prime}}^{\top}\boldsymbol{e}_{t}-\boldsymbol{e}_{l}^{\top}(\boldsymbol{e}_{t}^{\top}\otimes\boldsymbol{X})\boldsymbol{M}^{\dagger}(\boldsymbol{e}_{t^{\prime}}\otimes\boldsymbol{X}^{\top})\boldsymbol{e}_{i},

where the last equality is due to
\boldsymbol{M}^{\dagger}=(\boldsymbol{I}_{T}\otimes\boldsymbol{P}_{\hat{{S}}})\boldsymbol{M}^{\dagger}(\boldsymbol{I}_{T}\otimes\boldsymbol{P}_{\hat{{S}}})
.

Now the calculation of
\sum_{i=1}^{n}\frac{\partial F_{it}}{\partial E_{it^{\prime}}}
is straightforward,

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{n}\frac{\partial F_{it}}{\partial E_{it^{\prime}}}

\displaystyle=\sum_{i=1}^{n}\big[\boldsymbol{e}_{i}^{\top}\boldsymbol{e}_{i}\boldsymbol{e}_{t}^{\top}\boldsymbol{e}_{t^{\prime}}-\boldsymbol{e}_{i}^{\top}(\boldsymbol{e}_{t}^{\top}\otimes\boldsymbol{X})\boldsymbol{M}^{\dagger}(\boldsymbol{e}_{t^{\prime}}\otimes\boldsymbol{X}^{\top})\boldsymbol{e}_{i}\big]

\displaystyle=n\boldsymbol{e}_{t}^{\top}\boldsymbol{e}_{t^{\prime}}-\trace[(\boldsymbol{e}_{t}^{\top}\otimes\boldsymbol{X})\boldsymbol{M}^{\dagger}(\boldsymbol{e}_{t^{\prime}}\otimes\boldsymbol{X}^{\top})]

\displaystyle=n\boldsymbol{e}_{t}^{\top}\boldsymbol{e}_{t^{\prime}}-\boldsymbol{e}_{t}^{\top}\smash{\widehat{\mathbf{A}}}\boldsymbol{e}_{t^{\prime}}

\displaystyle=\boldsymbol{e}_{t}^{\top}(n\boldsymbol{I}_{T}-\smash{\widehat{\mathbf{A}}})\boldsymbol{e}_{t^{\prime}},

where the third equality is due to the formula of
\smash{\widehat{\mathbf{A}}}
in (
10
).

Noting that
\boldsymbol{F}=\boldsymbol{E}-\boldsymbol{Z}\boldsymbol{H}
, it follows that

\sum_{i=1}^{n}\frac{\partial\boldsymbol{e}_{i}^{\top}\boldsymbol{Z}\boldsymbol{H}\boldsymbol{e}_{t}}{\partial E_{it^{\prime}}}=\boldsymbol{e}_{t}^{\top}\smash{\widehat{\mathbf{A}}}\boldsymbol{e}_{t^{\prime}}
.

\blacksquare

H.4
Proofs of results in Appendix
F

Proof of
Lemma

F.3
.

Let
\boldsymbol{z}=\vec(\boldsymbol{E})
, then
\boldsymbol{z}\sim{\cal N}(\mathbf{0},\boldsymbol{K})
with
\boldsymbol{K}=\boldsymbol{S}\otimes\boldsymbol{I}_{n}
by Assumption
1
.
For each
t_{0},t_{0}^{\prime}\in[T]
, let

\boldsymbol{G}^{(t_{0},t_{0}^{\prime})}=\boldsymbol{F}\boldsymbol{e}_{t_{0}^{\prime}}\boldsymbol{e}_{t_{0}}^{\top}
, and
\boldsymbol{f}(\boldsymbol{z})^{(t_{0},t_{0}^{\prime})}=\vec(\boldsymbol{G}){\tilde{D}}^{-1}
.
For convenience, we will drop the superscript
{(t_{0},t_{0}^{\prime})}
from
\boldsymbol{G}^{(t_{0},t_{0}^{\prime})}
and
\boldsymbol{f}(\boldsymbol{z})^{(t_{0},t_{0}^{\prime})}
in this proof.
By
\trace(\boldsymbol{A}^{\top}\boldsymbol{B})=\vec(\boldsymbol{A})^{\top}\vec(\boldsymbol{B})
, we obtain

\displaystyle\boldsymbol{e}_{t_{0}}^{\top}\boldsymbol{E}^{\top}\boldsymbol{F}{\tilde{D}}^{-1}\boldsymbol{e}_{t_{0}^{\prime}}=\trace(\boldsymbol{E}^{\top}\boldsymbol{F}\boldsymbol{e}_{t_{0}^{\prime}}\boldsymbol{e}_{t_{0}}^{\top}){\tilde{D}}^{-1}=\trace(\boldsymbol{E}^{\top}\boldsymbol{G}{\tilde{D}}^{-1})=\boldsymbol{z}^{\top}\boldsymbol{f}(\boldsymbol{z}).

(39)

By product rule, we have

\displaystyle\nabla\boldsymbol{f}(\boldsymbol{z})=\frac{\partial\vec(\boldsymbol{G})}{\partial\vec(\boldsymbol{E})}{\tilde{D}}^{-1}+\underbrace{\vec(\boldsymbol{G})\frac{\partial{\tilde{D}}^{-1}}{\partial\vec(\boldsymbol{E})}}_{{\mathrm{Rem}}},

(40)

where
{\mathrm{Rem}}=\boldsymbol{u}\boldsymbol{v}^{\top}
with
\boldsymbol{u}=\vec(\boldsymbol{G})\in{\mathbb{R}}^{nT\times 1}
,
\boldsymbol{v}^{\top}=\frac{\partial{\tilde{D}}^{-1}}{\partial\vec(\boldsymbol{E})}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{1\times nT}
.
It follows that

\displaystyle\trace(\boldsymbol{K}\nabla\boldsymbol{f}(\boldsymbol{z}))=\trace\Big(\boldsymbol{K}\frac{\partial\vec(\boldsymbol{G})}{\partial\vec(\boldsymbol{E})}\Big){\tilde{D}}^{-1}+\trace(\boldsymbol{K}{\mathrm{Rem}}).

(41)

Since
\boldsymbol{K}=\boldsymbol{S}\otimes\boldsymbol{I}_{n}
and
\boldsymbol{G}=\boldsymbol{F}\boldsymbol{e}_{t_{0}^{\prime}}\boldsymbol{e}_{t_{0}}^{\top}
,

\boldsymbol{K}_{it,lt^{\prime}}=S_{tt^{\prime}}I(i=l)
, and
G_{it}=F_{it_{0}^{\prime}}I(t=t_{0})
. It follows

\displaystyle\trace\Big(\boldsymbol{K}\frac{\partial\vec(\boldsymbol{G})}{\partial\vec(\boldsymbol{E})}\Big)=\sum_{i,t}\sum_{l,t^{\prime}}\boldsymbol{K}_{it,lt^{\prime}}\frac{\partial G_{it}}{\partial E_{lt^{\prime}}}=\sum_{t^{\prime}}S_{t_{0}t^{\prime}}\sum_{i}\frac{\partial F_{it_{0}^{\prime}}}{\partial E_{it^{\prime}}}=\boldsymbol{e}_{t_{0}}^{\top}\boldsymbol{S}(n\boldsymbol{I}_{T}-\smash{\widehat{\mathbf{A}}})\boldsymbol{e}_{t_{0}^{\prime}},

(42)

where the last equality used
Lemma

E.1
and that
\smash{\widehat{\mathbf{A}}}
is symmetric.

Now we rewrite the quantity we want to bound as

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}\Bigl[\lVert\boldsymbol{E}^{\top}\boldsymbol{F}/{\tilde{D}}-\boldsymbol{S}(n\boldsymbol{I}_{T}-\smash{\widehat{\mathbf{A}}})/{\tilde{D}}\rVert_{\rm{F}}^{2}\Bigr]

\displaystyle=~

\displaystyle\sum_{t_{0},t_{0}^{\prime}}{\mathbb{E}}\Big[\Big(\boldsymbol{e}_{t_{0}}^{\top}\boldsymbol{E}^{\top}\boldsymbol{F}{\tilde{D}}^{-1}\boldsymbol{e}_{t_{0}^{\prime}}-\boldsymbol{e}_{t_{0}}^{\top}\boldsymbol{S}(n\boldsymbol{I}_{T}-\smash{\widehat{\mathbf{A}}})\boldsymbol{e}_{t_{0}^{\prime}}{\tilde{D}}^{-1}\Big)^{2}\Big]

\displaystyle=~

\displaystyle\sum_{t_{0},t_{0}^{\prime}}{\mathbb{E}}\Big[\big(\boldsymbol{z}^{\top}\boldsymbol{f}(\boldsymbol{z})-\trace(\boldsymbol{K}\nabla\boldsymbol{f}(\boldsymbol{z}))+\trace(\boldsymbol{K}{\mathrm{Rem}})\big)^{2}\Big]

\displaystyle\leq~

\displaystyle 2\sum_{t_{0},t_{0}^{\prime}}\Big\{{\mathbb{E}}\Big[\big(\boldsymbol{z}^{\top}\boldsymbol{f}(\boldsymbol{z})-\trace(\boldsymbol{K}\nabla\boldsymbol{f}(\boldsymbol{z}))\big)^{2}\Big]+{\mathbb{E}}\Big[\big(\trace(\boldsymbol{K}{\mathrm{Rem}})\big)^{2}\Big]\Big\},

(43)

where the second equality follows from (
39
), (
41
) and (
42
), and the last inequality uses elementary inequality
(a+b)^{2}\leq 2(a^{2}+b^{2})
.
We next bound the two terms in (
43
).

First term in (
43
).

By second-order Stein formula in
Lemma

F.1
,

\sum_{t_{0},t_{0}^{\prime}}{\mathbb{E}}\big(\boldsymbol{z}^{\top}\boldsymbol{f}(\boldsymbol{z})-\trace(\boldsymbol{K}\nabla\boldsymbol{f}(\boldsymbol{z}))\big)^{2}=\sum_{t_{0},t_{0}^{\prime}}{\mathbb{E}}\Big[\lVert\boldsymbol{K}^{\frac{1}{2}}\boldsymbol{f}(\boldsymbol{z})\rVert_{\rm{F}}^{2}+\trace\big[\big(\boldsymbol{K}\nabla\boldsymbol{f}(\boldsymbol{z})\big)^{2}\big]\Big].

(44)

Now we bound the two terms in the right-hand side of (
44
).
For the first term, recall
\boldsymbol{f}(\boldsymbol{z})=\vec(\boldsymbol{G}){\tilde{D}}^{-1}
, and
\boldsymbol{G}=\boldsymbol{F}\boldsymbol{e}_{t_{0}^{\prime}}\boldsymbol{e}_{t_{0}}^{\top}
, we obtain

\displaystyle\lVert\boldsymbol{K}^{\frac{1}{2}}\boldsymbol{f}(\boldsymbol{z})\rVert_{\rm{F}}^{2}={\tilde{D}}^{-2}\lVert(\boldsymbol{S}^{\frac{1}{2}}\otimes\boldsymbol{I}_{n})\vec(\boldsymbol{G})\rVert_{\rm{F}}^{2}={\tilde{D}}^{-2}\lVert\boldsymbol{G}\boldsymbol{S}^{\frac{1}{2}}\rVert_{\rm{F}}^{2}={\tilde{D}}^{-2}\lVert\boldsymbol{S}^{\frac{1}{2}}\boldsymbol{e}_{t_{0}}\rVert_{\rm{F}}^{2}\lVert\boldsymbol{F}\boldsymbol{e}_{t_{0}^{\prime}}\rVert_{\rm{F}}^{2}.

Summing over all
(t_{0},t_{0}^{\prime})\in[T]\times[T]
, we obtain

\sum_{t_{0},t_{0}^{\prime}}\lVert\boldsymbol{K}^{\frac{1}{2}}\boldsymbol{f}(\boldsymbol{z})\rVert_{\rm{F}}^{2}={\tilde{D}}^{-2}\lVert\boldsymbol{F}\rVert_{\rm{F}}^{2}\trace(\boldsymbol{S}).

(45)

For the second term in RHS of (
44
), recall
\nabla\boldsymbol{f}(\boldsymbol{z})=\frac{\partial\vec(\boldsymbol{G})}{\partial\vec(\boldsymbol{E})}{\tilde{D}}^{-1}+{\mathrm{Rem}}
,

\displaystyle\trace\big[\big(\boldsymbol{K}\nabla\boldsymbol{f}(\boldsymbol{z})\big)^{2}\big]

\displaystyle=~

\displaystyle{\tilde{D}}^{-2}\trace\Big[\Big(\boldsymbol{K}\frac{\partial\vec(\boldsymbol{G})}{\partial\vec(\boldsymbol{E})}\Big)^{2}\Big]+\trace[(\boldsymbol{K}{\mathrm{Rem}})^{2}]+2{\tilde{D}}^{-1}\trace\Big[\boldsymbol{K}\frac{\partial\vec(\boldsymbol{G})}{\partial\vec(\boldsymbol{E})}\boldsymbol{K}{\mathrm{Rem}}\Big].

(46)

By property of vectorization operation,

\vec(\boldsymbol{G})=\vec(\boldsymbol{F}\boldsymbol{e}_{t_{0}^{\prime}}\boldsymbol{e}_{t_{0}}^{\top})=(\boldsymbol{e}_{t_{0}}\boldsymbol{e}_{t_{0}^{\prime}}^{\top}\otimes\boldsymbol{I}_{n})\vec(\boldsymbol{F})
,
hence

\frac{\partial\vec(\boldsymbol{G})}{\partial\vec(\boldsymbol{E})}=(\boldsymbol{e}_{t_{0}}\boldsymbol{e}_{t_{0}^{\prime}}^{\top}\otimes\boldsymbol{I}_{n})\frac{\partial\vec(\boldsymbol{F})}{\partial\vec(\boldsymbol{E})},

where
\lVert\frac{\partial\vec(\boldsymbol{F})}{\partial\vec(\boldsymbol{E})}\rVert_{\rm{op}}\leq 1
since the map
\vec(\boldsymbol{E})\mapsto\vec(\boldsymbol{F})
is 1-Lipschitz by
[
6
, proposition 3]
.

Now we bound the three terms in (
46
). For the first term, by Cauchy-Schwarz inequality,

\displaystyle{\tilde{D}}^{-2}\trace\Big[\Big(\boldsymbol{K}\frac{\partial\vec(\boldsymbol{G})}{\partial\vec(\boldsymbol{E})}\Big)^{2}\Big]

\displaystyle=~

\displaystyle{\tilde{D}}^{-2}\trace\Big(\boldsymbol{K}(\boldsymbol{e}_{t_{0}}\boldsymbol{e}_{t_{0}^{\prime}}^{\top}\otimes\boldsymbol{I}_{n})\frac{\partial\vec(\boldsymbol{F})}{\partial\vec(\boldsymbol{E})}\boldsymbol{K}(\boldsymbol{e}_{t_{0}}\boldsymbol{e}_{t_{0}^{\prime}}^{\top}\otimes\boldsymbol{I}_{n})\frac{\partial\vec(\boldsymbol{F})}{\partial\vec(\boldsymbol{E})}\Big)

\displaystyle\leq~

\displaystyle{\tilde{D}}^{-2}\lVert(\boldsymbol{e}_{t_{0}^{\prime}}^{\top}\otimes\boldsymbol{I}_{n})\frac{\partial\vec(\boldsymbol{F})}{\partial\vec(\boldsymbol{E})}\boldsymbol{K}(\boldsymbol{e}_{t_{0}}\otimes\boldsymbol{I}_{n})\rVert_{\rm{F}}^{2}.

For the second term in (
46
), recall
{\mathrm{Rem}}=\boldsymbol{u}\boldsymbol{v}^{\top}
, and
\boldsymbol{u}=\vec(\boldsymbol{G})
,
\boldsymbol{v}^{\top}=\frac{\partial{\tilde{D}}^{-1}}{\partial\vec(\boldsymbol{E})}
from (
40
), then
\trace[(\boldsymbol{K}{\mathrm{Rem}})^{2}]=\trace(\boldsymbol{K}\boldsymbol{u}\boldsymbol{v}^{\top}\boldsymbol{K}\boldsymbol{u}\boldsymbol{v}^{\top})=(\boldsymbol{v}^{\top}\boldsymbol{K}\boldsymbol{u})^{2}
, thus,

\displaystyle\trace[(\boldsymbol{K}{\mathrm{Rem}})^{2}]

\displaystyle=~

\displaystyle\Big[\frac{\partial{\tilde{D}}^{-1}}{\partial\vec(\boldsymbol{E})}\boldsymbol{K}\vec(\boldsymbol{G})\Big]^{2}

\displaystyle=~

\displaystyle{\tilde{D}}^{-6}\Big[\vec(\boldsymbol{F})^{\top}\frac{\partial\vec(\boldsymbol{F})}{\partial\vec(\boldsymbol{E})}\boldsymbol{K}(\boldsymbol{e}_{t_{0}}\boldsymbol{e}_{t_{0}^{\prime}}^{\top}\otimes\boldsymbol{I}_{n})\vec(\boldsymbol{F})\Big]^{2}

\displaystyle\leq~

\displaystyle{\tilde{D}}^{-6}\lVert\vec(\boldsymbol{F})^{\top}\frac{\partial\vec(\boldsymbol{F})}{\partial\vec(\boldsymbol{E})}\boldsymbol{K}(\boldsymbol{e}_{t_{0}}\otimes\boldsymbol{I}_{n})\rVert^{2}\lVert(\boldsymbol{e}_{t_{0}^{\prime}}^{\top}\otimes\boldsymbol{I}_{n})\vec(\boldsymbol{F})\rVert^{2}.

where the first inequality uses
\frac{\partial{\tilde{D}}^{-1}}{\partial\vec(\boldsymbol{E})}=-\frac{1}{2}{\tilde{D}}^{-3}\frac{\partial\lVert\boldsymbol{F}\rVert_{\rm{F}}^{2}}{\partial\vec(\boldsymbol{E})}=-{\tilde{D}}^{-3}\vec(\boldsymbol{F})^{\top}\frac{\partial\vec(\boldsymbol{F})}{\partial\vec(\boldsymbol{E})}
by chain rule, and the inequality uses Cauchy-Schwarz inequality.

For the third term in (
46
), recall
{\mathrm{Rem}}=\boldsymbol{u}\boldsymbol{v}^{\top}
, and
\boldsymbol{u}=\vec(\boldsymbol{G})
,
\boldsymbol{v}^{\top}=\frac{\partial{\tilde{D}}^{-1}}{\partial\vec(\boldsymbol{E})}
, then

\trace\Big[\boldsymbol{K}\frac{\partial\vec(\boldsymbol{G})}{\partial\vec(\boldsymbol{E})}\boldsymbol{K}{\mathrm{Rem}}\Big]=\boldsymbol{v}^{\top}\boldsymbol{K}\frac{\partial\vec(\boldsymbol{G})}{\partial\vec(\boldsymbol{E})}\boldsymbol{K}\boldsymbol{u}
, hence

\displaystyle 2{\tilde{D}}^{-1}\trace\Big[\boldsymbol{K}\frac{\partial\vec(\boldsymbol{G})}{\partial\vec(\boldsymbol{E})}\boldsymbol{K}{\mathrm{Rem}}\Big]

\displaystyle=~

\displaystyle 2{\tilde{D}}^{-1}\frac{\partial{\tilde{D}}^{-1}}{\partial\vec(\boldsymbol{E})}\boldsymbol{K}\frac{\partial\vec(\boldsymbol{G})}{\partial\vec(\boldsymbol{E})}\boldsymbol{K}\vec(\boldsymbol{G})

\displaystyle=~

\displaystyle-2{\tilde{D}}^{-4}\vec(\boldsymbol{F})^{\top}\frac{\partial\vec(\boldsymbol{F})}{\partial\vec(\boldsymbol{E})}\boldsymbol{K}(\boldsymbol{e}_{t_{0}}\boldsymbol{e}_{t_{0}^{\prime}}^{\top}\otimes\boldsymbol{I}_{n})\frac{\partial\vec(\boldsymbol{F})}{\partial\vec(\boldsymbol{E})}\boldsymbol{K}(\boldsymbol{e}_{t_{0}}\boldsymbol{e}_{t_{0}^{\prime}}^{\top}\otimes\boldsymbol{I}_{n})\vec(\boldsymbol{F})

\displaystyle\leq~

\displaystyle 2{\tilde{D}}^{-4}\lVert(\boldsymbol{e}_{t_{0}^{\prime}}^{\top}\otimes\boldsymbol{I}_{n})\vec(\boldsymbol{F})\vec(\boldsymbol{F})^{\top}\frac{\partial\vec(\boldsymbol{F})}{\partial\vec(\boldsymbol{E})}\boldsymbol{K}(\boldsymbol{e}_{t_{0}}\otimes\boldsymbol{I}_{n})\rVert_{\rm{F}}

\displaystyle\lVert(\boldsymbol{e}_{t_{0}^{\prime}}^{\top}\otimes\boldsymbol{I}_{n})\frac{\partial\vec(\boldsymbol{F})}{\partial\vec(\boldsymbol{E})}\boldsymbol{K}(\boldsymbol{e}_{t_{0}}\otimes\boldsymbol{I}_{n})\rVert_{\rm{F}},

where the last inequality uses Cauchy-Schwarz inequality.

Summing over all
(t_{0},t_{0}^{\prime})\in[T]\times[T]
for these three terms in (
46
), using
\lVert\frac{\partial\vec(\boldsymbol{F})}{\partial\vec(\boldsymbol{E})}\rVert_{\rm{op}}\leq 1
,
\boldsymbol{K}=\boldsymbol{S}\otimes\boldsymbol{I}_{n}
, and
\lVert\boldsymbol{S}\rVert_{\rm{F}}\leq\lVert\boldsymbol{S}^{\frac{1}{2}}\rVert_{\rm{F}}^{2}=\trace(\boldsymbol{S})
, we obtain

\displaystyle\sum_{t_{0},t_{0}^{\prime}}\trace\big[\big(\boldsymbol{K}\nabla\boldsymbol{f}(\boldsymbol{z})\big)^{2}\big]

\displaystyle\leq~

\displaystyle{\tilde{D}}^{-2}\lVert\boldsymbol{K}\rVert_{\rm{F}}^{2}+{\tilde{D}}^{-6}\lVert\boldsymbol{F}\rVert_{\rm{F}}^{2}\lVert\boldsymbol{K}\rVert_{\rm{F}}^{2}\lVert\boldsymbol{F}\rVert_{\rm{F}}^{2}+2{\tilde{D}}^{-4}\lVert\boldsymbol{F}\rVert_{\rm{F}}^{2}\lVert\boldsymbol{K}\rVert_{\rm{F}}^{2}

\displaystyle=~

\displaystyle{\tilde{D}}^{-2}n\lVert\boldsymbol{S}\rVert_{\rm{F}}^{2}+{\tilde{D}}^{-6}\lVert\boldsymbol{F}\rVert_{\rm{F}}^{4}n\lVert\boldsymbol{S}\rVert_{\rm{F}}^{2}+2{\tilde{D}}^{-4}\lVert\boldsymbol{F}\rVert_{\rm{F}}^{2}n\lVert\boldsymbol{S}\rVert_{\rm{F}}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq~

\displaystyle\big[{\tilde{D}}^{-2}n\trace(\boldsymbol{S})+{\tilde{D}}^{-6}\lVert\boldsymbol{F}\rVert_{\rm{F}}^{4}n\trace(\boldsymbol{S})+2{\tilde{D}}^{-4}\lVert\boldsymbol{F}\rVert_{\rm{F}}^{2}n\trace(\boldsymbol{S})\big]\trace(\boldsymbol{S}).

(47)

Second term in (
43
).

Recall that
{\mathrm{Rem}}=\boldsymbol{u}\boldsymbol{v}^{\top}
, hence
[\trace(\boldsymbol{K}{\mathrm{Rem}})]^{2}=\trace[(\boldsymbol{K}{\mathrm{Rem}})^{2}]
.
By calculation of second term in (
46
), we obtain

\displaystyle\sum_{t_{0},t_{0}^{\prime}}[\trace(\boldsymbol{K}{\mathrm{Rem}})]^{2}=\sum_{t_{0},t_{0}^{\prime}}\trace[(\boldsymbol{K}{\mathrm{Rem}})^{2}]\leq{\tilde{D}}^{-6}\lVert\boldsymbol{F}\rVert_{\rm{F}}^{4}n\trace(\boldsymbol{S})^{2}.

(48)

Combining the results (
43
), (
44
), (
45
), (
47
), (
48
), we obtain

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}\Bigl[\lVert\boldsymbol{E}^{\top}\boldsymbol{F}/{\tilde{D}}-\boldsymbol{S}(n\boldsymbol{I}_{T}-\smash{\widehat{\mathbf{A}}})/{\tilde{D}}\rVert_{\rm{F}}^{2}\Bigr]

\displaystyle\leq~

\displaystyle 2\big[{\tilde{D}}^{-2}\lVert\boldsymbol{F}\rVert_{\rm{F}}^{2}+{\tilde{D}}^{-2}n\trace(\boldsymbol{S})+2{\tilde{D}}^{-6}\lVert\boldsymbol{F}\rVert_{\rm{F}}^{4}n\trace(\boldsymbol{S})+2{\tilde{D}}^{-4}\lVert\boldsymbol{F}\rVert_{\rm{F}}^{2}n\trace(\boldsymbol{S})\big]\trace(\boldsymbol{S})

\displaystyle\leq~

\displaystyle 4\trace(\boldsymbol{S}),

thanks to
{\tilde{D}}^{2}=\lVert\boldsymbol{F}\rVert_{\rm{F}}^{2}+n\trace(\boldsymbol{S})
.

\blacksquare

Proof of
Lemma

F.4
.

Apply
[
4
, Proposition 6.3]
with
\boldsymbol{\rho}=\boldsymbol{U}\boldsymbol{e}_{t},\boldsymbol{\eta}=\boldsymbol{V}\boldsymbol{e}_{t^{\prime}}
, we obtain

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}\Big[\Big\lVert\boldsymbol{U}^{\top}\boldsymbol{Z}\boldsymbol{V}-\sum_{j=1}^{p}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\frac{\partial}{\partial z_{ij}}\Bigl(\boldsymbol{U}^{\top}\boldsymbol{e}_{i}\boldsymbol{e}_{j}^{\top}\boldsymbol{V}\Bigr)\Big\rVert_{\rm F}^{2}\Big]

\displaystyle=~

\displaystyle\sum_{t,t^{\prime}=1}^{T}{\mathbb{E}}\big(\boldsymbol{e}_{t}^{\top}\boldsymbol{U}^{\top}\boldsymbol{Z}\boldsymbol{V}\boldsymbol{e}_{t^{\prime}}-\sum_{j=1}^{p}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\frac{\partial}{\partial z_{ij}}\boldsymbol{e}_{t}\boldsymbol{U}^{\top}\boldsymbol{e}_{i}\boldsymbol{e}_{j}^{\top}\boldsymbol{V}\boldsymbol{e}_{t^{\prime}}\big)^{2}

\displaystyle\leq~

\displaystyle\sum_{t,t^{\prime}=1}^{T}\left[{\mathbb{E}}\big[\left\lVert\boldsymbol{U}\boldsymbol{e}_{t}\right\rVert^{2}\left\lVert\boldsymbol{V}\boldsymbol{e}_{t^{\prime}}\right\rVert^{2}\big]+{\mathbb{E}}\sum_{ij}\Big[2\left\lVert\boldsymbol{V}\boldsymbol{e}_{t^{\prime}}\right\rVert^{2}\left\lVert\frac{\partial\boldsymbol{U}\boldsymbol{e}_{t}}{\partial z_{ij}}\right\rVert_{\rm{F}}+2\left\lVert\boldsymbol{U}\boldsymbol{e}_{t}\right\rVert^{2}\left\lVert\frac{\partial\boldsymbol{V}\boldsymbol{e}_{t^{\prime}}}{\partial z_{ij}}\right\rVert_{\rm{F}}\Big]\right]

\displaystyle=~

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}\left\lVert\boldsymbol{U}\right\rVert_{\rm{F}}^{2}\left\lVert\boldsymbol{V}\right\rVert_{\rm{F}}^{2}+{\mathbb{E}}\sum_{ij}\Big[2\left\lVert\boldsymbol{V}\right\rVert_{\rm{F}}^{2}\left\lVert\frac{\partial\boldsymbol{U}}{\partial z_{ij}}\right\rVert_{\rm{F}}^{2}+2\left\lVert\boldsymbol{U}\right\rVert_{\rm{F}}^{2}\left\lVert\frac{\partial\boldsymbol{V}}{\partial z_{ij}}\right\rVert_{\rm{F}}^{2}\Big].

\blacksquare

Proof of
Corollary

F.5
.

By Kirszbraun’s theorem, there exists an
L_{1}
-Lipschitz function
\bar{\boldsymbol{U}}:{\mathbb{R}}^{n\times p}\to{\mathbb{R}}^{n\times T}
such that
\bar{\boldsymbol{U}}=\boldsymbol{U}
on
\Omega
, and an
L_{2}
-Lipschitz function
\bar{\boldsymbol{V}}:{\mathbb{R}}^{n\times p}\to{\mathbb{R}}^{n\times T}
such that
\bar{\boldsymbol{V}}=\boldsymbol{V}
on
\Omega
. By projecting
\bar{\boldsymbol{U}}
and
\bar{\boldsymbol{V}}
onto the Euclidean ball of radius 1 and
K
if necessary, we assume without loss of generality that
\lVert\bar{\boldsymbol{U}}\rVert_{\rm{F}}\leq 1
and
\lVert\bar{\boldsymbol{V}}\rVert_{\rm{F}}\leq K
.
Therefore,

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}\Big[I(\Omega)\lVert\boldsymbol{U}^{\top}\boldsymbol{Z}\boldsymbol{V}-\sum_{j=1}^{p}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\frac{\partial}{\partial z_{ij}}\Bigl(\boldsymbol{U}^{\top}\boldsymbol{e}_{i}\boldsymbol{e}_{j}^{\top}\boldsymbol{V}\Bigr)\rVert_{\rm{F}}^{2}\Big]

\displaystyle=~

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}\Big[I(\Omega)\lVert\bar{\boldsymbol{U}}^{\top}\boldsymbol{Z}\bar{\boldsymbol{V}}-\sum_{j=1}^{p}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\frac{\partial}{\partial z_{ij}}\Bigl(\bar{\boldsymbol{U}}^{\top}\boldsymbol{e}_{i}\boldsymbol{e}_{j}^{\top}\bar{\boldsymbol{V}}\Bigr)\rVert_{\rm{F}}^{2}\Big]

\displaystyle\leq~

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}\Big[\lVert\bar{\boldsymbol{U}}^{\top}\boldsymbol{Z}\bar{\boldsymbol{V}}-\sum_{j=1}^{p}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\frac{\partial}{\partial z_{ij}}\Bigl(\bar{\boldsymbol{U}}^{\top}\boldsymbol{e}_{i}\boldsymbol{e}_{j}^{\top}\bar{\boldsymbol{V}}\Bigr)\rVert_{\rm{F}}^{2}\Big]

\displaystyle\leq~

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}\Big[\big(\lVert\bar{\boldsymbol{U}}\rVert_{\rm{F}}^{2}\lVert\bar{\boldsymbol{V}}\rVert_{\rm{F}}^{2}\big)+2\sum_{ij}\Big(\lVert\bar{\boldsymbol{V}}\rVert_{\rm{F}}^{2}\left\lVert\frac{\partial\bar{\boldsymbol{U}}}{\partial z_{ij}}\right\rVert_{\rm{F}}^{2}+\lVert\bar{\boldsymbol{U}}\rVert_{\rm{F}}^{2}\left\lVert\frac{\partial\bar{\boldsymbol{V}}}{\partial z_{ij}}\right\rVert_{\rm{F}}^{2}\Big)\Big]

\displaystyle\leq~

\displaystyle K^{2}+2{\mathbb{E}}\Big[\sum_{ij}\Big(K^{2}\left\lVert\frac{\partial\bar{\boldsymbol{U}}}{\partial z_{ij}}\right\rVert_{\rm{F}}^{2}+\left\lVert\frac{\partial\bar{\boldsymbol{V}}}{\partial z_{ij}}\right\rVert_{\rm{F}}^{2}\Big)\Big]

\displaystyle=~

\displaystyle K^{2}+2{\mathbb{E}}\Big[I(\Omega)\sum_{ij}\Big(K^{2}\left\lVert\frac{\partial\bar{\boldsymbol{U}}}{\partial z_{ij}}\right\rVert_{\rm{F}}^{2}+\left\lVert\frac{\partial\bar{\boldsymbol{V}}}{\partial z_{ij}}\right\rVert_{\rm{F}}^{2}\Big)+I(\Omega^{c})\sum_{ij}\Big(K^{2}\left\lVert\frac{\partial\bar{\boldsymbol{U}}}{\partial z_{ij}}\right\rVert_{\rm{F}}^{2}+\left\lVert\frac{\partial\bar{\boldsymbol{V}}}{\partial z_{ij}}\right\rVert_{\rm{F}}^{2}\Big)\Big]

\displaystyle=~

\displaystyle K^{2}+2{\mathbb{E}}\Big[I(\Omega)\sum_{ij}\Big(K^{2}\left\lVert\frac{\partial\boldsymbol{U}}{\partial z_{ij}}\right\rVert_{\rm{F}}^{2}+\left\lVert\frac{\partial\boldsymbol{V}}{\partial z_{ij}}\right\rVert_{\rm{F}}^{2}\Big)+I(\Omega^{c})\sum_{ij}\Big(K^{2}\left\lVert\frac{\partial\bar{\boldsymbol{U}}}{\partial z_{ij}}\right\rVert_{\rm{F}}^{2}+\left\lVert\frac{\partial\bar{\boldsymbol{V}}}{\partial z_{ij}}\right\rVert_{\rm{F}}^{2}\Big)\Big]

\displaystyle\leq~

\displaystyle K^{2}+2{\mathbb{E}}\Big[I(\Omega)\sum_{ij}\Big(K^{2}\left\lVert\frac{\partial\boldsymbol{U}}{\partial z_{ij}}\right\rVert_{\rm{F}}^{2}+\left\lVert\frac{\partial\boldsymbol{V}}{\partial z_{ij}}\right\rVert_{\rm{F}}^{2}\Big)\Big]+2C(K^{2}L_{1}^{2}+L_{2}^{2}),

where the last inequality uses
\sum_{ij}\lVert\frac{\partial\bar{\boldsymbol{U}}}{\partial z_{ij}}\rVert_{\rm{F}}^{2}\leq nT(n^{-1/2}L_{1})^{2}=TL_{1}^{2}
,
\sum_{ij}\lVert\frac{\partial\bar{\boldsymbol{V}}}{\partial z_{ij}}\rVert_{\rm{F}}^{2}\leq TL_{2}^{2}
by Lipschitz properties of
\bar{\boldsymbol{U}}
,
\bar{\boldsymbol{V}}
, and
P(\Omega^{c})\leq C/T
.

\blacksquare

Proof of
Lemma

F.6
.

For each
j\in[p]
, let
{\mathbb{E}}_{j}(\cdot)
denote the conditional expectation
{\mathbb{E}}[\cdot|\{\boldsymbol{Z}e_{k},k\neq j\}]
. The left-hand side of the desired inequality can be rewritten as

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}\Big[\big\lVert p\boldsymbol{U}^{\top}\boldsymbol{V}-\sum_{j=1}^{p}({\mathbb{E}}_{j}\boldsymbol{U}^{\top}\boldsymbol{Z}-\boldsymbol{L}^{\top})\boldsymbol{e}_{j}\boldsymbol{e}_{j}^{\top}(\boldsymbol{Z}^{\top}{\mathbb{E}}_{j}\boldsymbol{V}-\hat{\boldsymbol{L}})\big\rVert_{\rm{F}}\Big]

with
\boldsymbol{L}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{p\times T}
defined by

\boldsymbol{L}^{\top}\boldsymbol{e}_{j}={\mathbb{E}}_{j}\boldsymbol{U}^{\top}\boldsymbol{Z}\boldsymbol{e}_{j}-\boldsymbol{U}^{\top}\boldsymbol{Z}\boldsymbol{e}_{j}+\sum_{i=1}^{n}\partial_{ij}\boldsymbol{U}^{\top}\boldsymbol{e}_{i}

and
\hat{\boldsymbol{L}}
defined similarly with
\boldsymbol{U}
replaced by
\boldsymbol{V}
.
We develop the terms in the sum over
j
as follows:

\displaystyle p\boldsymbol{U}^{\top}\boldsymbol{V}-\sum_{j}({\mathbb{E}}_{j}\boldsymbol{U}^{\top}\boldsymbol{Z}-\boldsymbol{L}^{\top})\boldsymbol{e}_{j}\boldsymbol{e}_{j}^{\top}(\boldsymbol{Z}^{\top}{\mathbb{E}}_{j}\boldsymbol{V}-\hat{\boldsymbol{L}})

\displaystyle=\quad

\displaystyle\sum_{j}\Bigl(\boldsymbol{U}^{\top}\boldsymbol{V}-{\mathbb{E}}_{j}[\boldsymbol{U}^{\top}]{\mathbb{E}}_{j}\boldsymbol{V}\Bigr)

(49)

\displaystyle+\sum_{j}\Bigl({\mathbb{E}}_{j}[\boldsymbol{U}^{\top}]{\mathbb{E}}_{j}\boldsymbol{V}-{\mathbb{E}}_{j}\boldsymbol{U}^{\top}\boldsymbol{Z}\boldsymbol{e}_{j}\boldsymbol{e}_{j}^{\top}\boldsymbol{Z}^{\top}{\mathbb{E}}_{j}\boldsymbol{V}\Bigr)

(50)

\displaystyle-\boldsymbol{L}^{\top}\hat{\boldsymbol{L}}

(51)

\displaystyle+\sum_{j}\Bigl({\mathbb{E}}_{j}\boldsymbol{U}^{\top}\boldsymbol{Z}\boldsymbol{e}_{j}\boldsymbol{e}_{j}^{\top}\hat{\boldsymbol{L}}\Bigr)+\Bigl(\boldsymbol{L}^{\top}\boldsymbol{e}_{j}\boldsymbol{e}_{j}^{\top}\boldsymbol{Z}^{\top}{\mathbb{E}}_{j}[\boldsymbol{U}]\Bigr).

(52)

First, for (
51
),
by the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality

{\mathbb{E}}\bigl[\|\boldsymbol{L}^{\top}\hat{\boldsymbol{L}}\|_{\rm F}\bigr]\leq{\mathbb{E}}\bigl[\|\boldsymbol{L}\|_{\rm F}^{2}\bigr]^{\frac{1}{2}}{\mathbb{E}}\bigl[\|\hat{\boldsymbol{L}}\|_{\rm F}^{2}\bigr]^{\frac{1}{2}}
.
For a fixed
j\in[p]
and
t\in[T]
,

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}[(\boldsymbol{e}_{j}^{\top}\boldsymbol{L}e_{t})^{2}]

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{i=1}^{n}{\mathbb{E}}[(\boldsymbol{e}_{i}^{\top}({\mathbb{E}}_{j}[\boldsymbol{U}]-\boldsymbol{U})e_{t})^{2}]+{\mathbb{E}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{l=1}^{n}\Bigl(\frac{\boldsymbol{e}_{i}^{\top}\partial\boldsymbol{U}e_{t}}{\partial z_{lj}}\Bigr)^{2}

\displaystyle\leq 2{\mathbb{E}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{l=1}^{n}\Bigl(\frac{\boldsymbol{e}_{i}^{\top}\partial\boldsymbol{U}e_{t}}{\partial z_{lj}}\Bigr)^{2},

where the two inequalities are due to the second-order stein inequality in
Lemma

F.1
, and Gaussian-Poincaré inequality in
Lemma

F.2
, respectively.
Summing over
j\in[p]
and
t\in[T]
we obtain

{\mathbb{E}}[\|\boldsymbol{L}\|_{\rm F}^{2}]\leq 2{\mathbb{E}}\sum_{lj}\|\partial_{lj}\boldsymbol{U}\|_{\rm F}^{2}=2\|\boldsymbol{U}\|_{\partial}^{2}
.
Combined with the same bound for
\hat{\boldsymbol{L}}
, we obtain

{\mathbb{E}}[\|\eqref{eq:Xi-hat-Xi-term}\|_{\rm F}^{2}]\leq 2\|\boldsymbol{U}\|_{\partial}\|\boldsymbol{V}\|_{\partial}
.
We now turn to the two terms in (
52
).
By the triangle inequality for the Frobenius norm,

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}\Bigl[\|\sum_{j}{\mathbb{E}}_{j}\boldsymbol{U}^{\top}\boldsymbol{Z}\boldsymbol{e}_{j}\boldsymbol{e}_{j}^{\top}\hat{\boldsymbol{L}}\|_{\rm F}\Bigr]

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{j}{\mathbb{E}}\Bigl[\|{\mathbb{E}}_{j}\boldsymbol{U}^{\top}\boldsymbol{Z}\boldsymbol{e}_{j}\|_{2}\|\boldsymbol{e}_{j}^{\top}\hat{\boldsymbol{L}}\|_{2}\Bigr]

\displaystyle\leq{\mathbb{E}}[\sum_{j}\|{\mathbb{E}}_{j}\boldsymbol{U}^{\top}\boldsymbol{Z}\boldsymbol{e}_{j}\|_{2}^{2}]^{\frac{1}{2}}{\mathbb{E}}[\sum_{j}\|\boldsymbol{e}_{j}^{\top}\hat{\boldsymbol{L}}\|_{2}^{2}]^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\leq(p{\mathbb{E}}[\lVert\boldsymbol{U}\rVert_{\rm{F}}^{2}])^{\frac{1}{2}}{\mathbb{E}}[\|\hat{\boldsymbol{L}}\|_{\rm F}^{2}]^{\frac{1}{2}}

where we used that
\|\boldsymbol{a}\boldsymbol{b}^{\top}\|_{\rm F}=\|\boldsymbol{a}\|_{2}\|\boldsymbol{b}\|_{2}
for two vectors
\boldsymbol{a},\boldsymbol{b}
,
the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality,
{\mathbb{E}}[\|\boldsymbol{A}\boldsymbol{z}_{j}\|_{2}^{2}|\boldsymbol{A}]=\|\boldsymbol{A}\|_{\rm F}^{2}

if matrix
\boldsymbol{A}
is independent of
\boldsymbol{z}_{j}\sim{\cal N}(0,I_{n})
(set
\boldsymbol{z}_{j}=\boldsymbol{Z}\boldsymbol{e}_{j}
),
and Jensen’s inequality.

Next, we decompose (
49
) as

\sum_{j}\boldsymbol{U}^{\top}(\boldsymbol{V}-{\mathbb{E}}_{j}\boldsymbol{V})+\sum_{j}(\boldsymbol{U}-{\mathbb{E}}_{j}\boldsymbol{U})^{\top}{\mathbb{E}}_{j}\boldsymbol{V}
.
We have by the submultiplicativity of the Frobenius norm
and the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}[\|\boldsymbol{U}^{\top}\sum_{j}(\boldsymbol{V}-{\mathbb{E}}_{j}\boldsymbol{V})\|_{\rm F}]

\displaystyle\leq{\mathbb{E}}[\sum_{j}\lVert\boldsymbol{U}\rVert_{\rm{F}}\|\boldsymbol{V}-{\mathbb{E}}_{j}\boldsymbol{V}\|_{\rm F}]

\displaystyle\leq{\mathbb{E}}[p\lVert\boldsymbol{U}\rVert_{\rm{F}}^{2}]^{\frac{1}{2}}{\mathbb{E}}[\sum_{j}\|\boldsymbol{V}-{\mathbb{E}}_{j}\boldsymbol{V}\|_{\rm F}^{2}]^{\frac{1}{2}}.

By the Gaussian Poincaré inequality applied
p
times,

{\mathbb{E}}[\sum_{j}\|\boldsymbol{V}-{\mathbb{E}}_{j}\boldsymbol{V}\|_{\rm F}^{2}]\leq\|\boldsymbol{V}\|_{\partial}^{2}
,
so that the previous display is bounded from above by
\sqrt{p}\|\boldsymbol{V}\|_{\partial}
.
Similarly,
{\mathbb{E}}[\|\sum_{j}({\mathbb{E}}_{j}[\boldsymbol{U}]-\boldsymbol{U})^{\top}{\mathbb{E}}_{j}\boldsymbol{V}\|_{\rm F}]\leq\sqrt{p}\|\boldsymbol{U}\|_{\partial}

and
{\mathbb{E}}[\|\eqref{eq:first-term-swap-trick}\|_{\rm F}]\leq\sqrt{p}(\|\boldsymbol{U}\|_{\partial}+\|\boldsymbol{V}\|_{\partial})
.

For the last remaining term, (
50
),
we first use

{\mathbb{E}}[\|\eqref{eq:difficult-term}\|_{\rm F}]\leq{\mathbb{E}}[\|\eqref{eq:difficult-term}\|_{\rm F}^{2}]^{\frac{1}{2}}
by Jensen’s inequality and now proceed to bound

\|\eqref{eq:difficult-term}\|_{\rm F}^{2}
. We have

\|\eqref{eq:difficult-term}\|_{\rm F}^{2}=\|\sum_{j}{\mathbb{E}}_{j}\boldsymbol{U}^{\top}{\mathbb{E}}_{j}\boldsymbol{V}-{\mathbb{E}}_{j}\boldsymbol{U}^{\top}X\boldsymbol{e}_{j}\boldsymbol{e}_{j}^{\top}\boldsymbol{Z}^{\top}{\mathbb{E}}_{j}\boldsymbol{V}\|_{\rm F}^{2}=\sum_{j,k}\trace[\boldsymbol{M}_{j}^{\top}\boldsymbol{M}_{k}],

where
\boldsymbol{M}_{j}={\mathbb{E}}_{j}\boldsymbol{U}^{\top}{\mathbb{E}}_{j}\boldsymbol{V}-{\mathbb{E}}_{j}\boldsymbol{U}^{\top}\boldsymbol{Z}\boldsymbol{e}_{j}\boldsymbol{e}_{j}^{\top}\boldsymbol{Z}^{\top}{\mathbb{E}}_{j}\boldsymbol{V}
.
We first bound
\sum_{j}\|\boldsymbol{M}_{j}\|_{\rm F}^{2}
.
Since the variance of
\boldsymbol{a}^{\top}\boldsymbol{b}-\boldsymbol{a}^{\top}\boldsymbol{g}\boldsymbol{g}^{\top}\boldsymbol{b}
for standard normal
\boldsymbol{g}\sim{\cal N}(0,I_{p})

is
2\|(\boldsymbol{a}\boldsymbol{b}^{\top}+\boldsymbol{b}\boldsymbol{a}^{\top})/2\|_{\rm F}^{2}\leq 2\|\boldsymbol{a}\|_{2}^{2}\|\boldsymbol{b}\|_{2}^{2}
,
applying this variance bound on each pair of coordinates
(t,t^{\prime})\in[T]\times[T]

gives
\sum_{j}\|\boldsymbol{M}_{j}\|_{\rm F}^{2}\leq\sum_{j}2\|{\mathbb{E}}_{j}[\boldsymbol{U}]\|_{\rm F}^{2}\|{\mathbb{E}}_{j}\boldsymbol{V}\|_{\rm F}^{2}\leq 2p
.

We now bound
\sum_{j\neq k}\trace[\boldsymbol{M}_{j}^{\top}\boldsymbol{M}_{k}]
.
Setting
\boldsymbol{z}_{j}=\boldsymbol{Z}\boldsymbol{e}_{j}\sim{\cal N}(0,I_{n})
for every
j\in[p]
,
we will use many times
the identity

{\mathbb{E}}[(\boldsymbol{z}_{j}^{\top}f(\boldsymbol{Z})-\sum_{i}\partial_{ij}f(\boldsymbol{Z})^{\top}\boldsymbol{e}_{i})g(\boldsymbol{Z})={\mathbb{E}}[\sum_{i}f(\boldsymbol{Z})^{\top}\boldsymbol{e}_{i}\partial_{ij}g(\boldsymbol{Z})]

(53)

which follows from Stein’s formula
for
f:{\mathbb{R}}^{n\times p}\to{\mathbb{R}}^{n}
and
g:{\mathbb{R}}^{n\times p}\to{\mathbb{R}}
.
With
f^{tt^{\prime}}(\boldsymbol{Z})=(\boldsymbol{z}_{j}^{\top}{\mathbb{E}}_{j}[\boldsymbol{U}]\boldsymbol{e}_{t^{\prime}}){\mathbb{E}}_{j}\boldsymbol{V}e_{t}

and
g^{tt^{\prime}}(\boldsymbol{Z})=\boldsymbol{e}_{t^{\prime}}^{\top}\boldsymbol{M}_{k}e_{t}
,
we find

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}\trace[\boldsymbol{M}_{j}^{\top}\boldsymbol{M}_{k}]={\mathbb{E}}\trace[\boldsymbol{M}_{j}^{\top}\sum_{t}\boldsymbol{e}_{t^{\prime}}\boldsymbol{e}_{t^{\prime}}^{\top}\boldsymbol{M}_{k}]={\mathbb{E}}[\sum_{tt^{\prime}}e_{t}^{\top}\boldsymbol{M}_{j}^{\top}\boldsymbol{e}_{t^{\prime}}\boldsymbol{e}_{t^{\prime}}^{\top}\boldsymbol{M}_{k}e_{t}]

\displaystyle={\mathbb{E}}\sum_{tt^{\prime}}\Bigl(\boldsymbol{z}_{j}^{\top}f^{tt^{\prime}}(\boldsymbol{Z})-\sum_{i}\boldsymbol{e}_{i}^{\top}\partial_{ij}f^{tt^{\prime}}(\boldsymbol{Z})\Bigr)g^{tt^{\prime}}(\boldsymbol{Z})

\displaystyle={\mathbb{E}}\sum_{tt^{\prime}}\sum_{i}\boldsymbol{e}_{i}^{\top}f^{tt^{\prime}}(\boldsymbol{Z})\partial_{ij}g^{tt^{\prime}}(\boldsymbol{Z}).

where
g_{tt^{\prime}}(\boldsymbol{Z})=(e_{t}^{\top}{\mathbb{E}}_{k}\boldsymbol{V}^{\top}{\mathbb{E}}_{k}\boldsymbol{V}e_{t}^{\prime}-e_{t}^{\top}{\mathbb{E}}_{k}\boldsymbol{U}^{\top}\boldsymbol{z}_{k}\boldsymbol{z}_{k}^{\top}{\mathbb{E}}_{k}\boldsymbol{V}e_{t}^{\prime})
and

\partial_{ij}g_{tt^{\prime}}=\partial_{ij}\boldsymbol{e}_{t^{\prime}}^{\top}\boldsymbol{M}_{k}e_{t}=\boldsymbol{e}_{t^{\prime}}^{\top}\partial_{ij}[{\mathbb{E}}_{k}\boldsymbol{U}^{\top}{\mathbb{E}}_{k}\boldsymbol{V}]e_{t}-\boldsymbol{z}_{k}^{\top}\partial_{ij}[{\mathbb{E}}_{k}\boldsymbol{U}\boldsymbol{e}_{t^{\prime}}\boldsymbol{e}_{t}^{\top}{\mathbb{E}}_{k}\boldsymbol{U}^{\top}]\boldsymbol{z}_{k}

Now define
\tilde{f}^{tt^{\prime}}(\boldsymbol{Z})=\partial_{ij}[{\mathbb{E}}_{k}\boldsymbol{U}\boldsymbol{e}_{t^{\prime}}\boldsymbol{e}_{t}^{\top}{\mathbb{E}}_{k}\boldsymbol{U}^{\top}]\boldsymbol{z}_{k}

and
\tilde{g}^{tt^{\prime}}(\boldsymbol{Z})=\sum_{i}\boldsymbol{e}_{i}^{\top}f^{tt^{\prime}}(\boldsymbol{Z})
.
By definition of
\tilde{f}^{tt^{\prime}}(\boldsymbol{Z})
,
the previous display is equal to

\boldsymbol{z}_{k}^{\top}\tilde{f}^{tt^{\prime}}(\boldsymbol{Z})-\sum_{l}\partial_{lk}\boldsymbol{e}_{l}^{\top}\tilde{f}^{tt^{\prime}}(\boldsymbol{Z})
.
We apply (
53
) again with respect to
\boldsymbol{z}_{k}
, so that

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}\trace[\boldsymbol{M}_{j}^{\top}\boldsymbol{M}_{k}]

\displaystyle=\sum_{il,tt^{\prime}}\boldsymbol{e}_{i}^{\top}\partial_{lk}[f^{tt^{\prime}}(\boldsymbol{Z})]\boldsymbol{e}_{l}^{\top}\tilde{f}^{tt^{\prime}}(\boldsymbol{Z})

\displaystyle=\sum_{il,tt^{\prime}}\Bigl(\boldsymbol{e}_{i}^{\top}\partial_{lk}\Bigl[{\mathbb{E}}_{j}\boldsymbol{V}e_{t}\boldsymbol{e}_{t^{\prime}}^{\top}{\mathbb{E}}_{j}\boldsymbol{U}^{\top}\Bigr]\boldsymbol{z}_{j}\Bigr)\Bigl(\boldsymbol{e}_{l}^{\top}\partial_{ij}\Bigl[{\mathbb{E}}_{k}[\boldsymbol{U}]\boldsymbol{e}_{t^{\prime}}e_{t}^{\top}{\mathbb{E}}_{k}[\boldsymbol{V}]^{\top}\Bigr]\boldsymbol{z}_{k}\Bigr).

To remove the indices
t,t^{\prime}
, we rewrite the above using
\sum_{t}\boldsymbol{e}_{t}\boldsymbol{e}_{t}^{\top}=I_{T}

and
\sum_{t^{\prime}}\boldsymbol{e}_{t^{\prime}}\boldsymbol{e}_{t^{\prime}}^{\top}=I_{T}
so that it equals

{\mathbb{E}}\sum_{il}\trace\Bigl\{\partial_{lk}\Bigl[{\mathbb{E}}_{j}\boldsymbol{U}^{\top}\boldsymbol{z}_{j}\boldsymbol{e}_{i}^{\top}{\mathbb{E}}_{j}\boldsymbol{V}\Bigr]\partial_{ij}\Bigl[{\mathbb{E}}_{k}[\boldsymbol{V}]^{\top}\boldsymbol{z}_{k}\boldsymbol{e}_{l}^{\top}{\mathbb{E}}_{k}[\boldsymbol{U}]\Bigr]\Bigr\}

Summing over
j,k
,
using
\trace[\boldsymbol{A}^{\top}\boldsymbol{B}]\leq\|\boldsymbol{A}\|_{\rm F}\|\boldsymbol{B}\|_{\rm F})

and the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality,
the above is bounded from above by

\displaystyle\Bigl\{{\mathbb{E}}\sum_{jk,il}\Big\|\partial_{lk}\Bigl[{\mathbb{E}}_{j}\boldsymbol{U}^{\top}\boldsymbol{z}_{j}\boldsymbol{e}_{i}^{\top}{\mathbb{E}}_{j}[\boldsymbol{V}]\Bigr]\Big\|_{\rm F}^{2}\Bigl\}^{\frac{1}{2}}\Bigl\{{\mathbb{E}}\sum_{jk,il}\Big\|\partial_{ij}\Bigl[{\mathbb{E}}_{k}[\boldsymbol{V}]^{\top}\boldsymbol{z}_{k}\boldsymbol{e}_{l}^{\top}{\mathbb{E}}_{k}[\boldsymbol{U}]\Bigr]\Big\|_{\rm F}^{2}\Bigr\}^{\frac{1}{2}}.

At this point the two factors are symmetric,
with
(\boldsymbol{V},\boldsymbol{U})
in the left factor
replaced with
(\boldsymbol{U},\boldsymbol{V})
on the right factor.
We focus on the left factor; similar bound will
apply to the right one by exchanging the roles of
\boldsymbol{V}
and
\boldsymbol{U}
.
If
\boldsymbol{z}_{j}
is independent of matrices
A^{(q)}

{\mathbb{E}}_{j}[\|\sum_{q=1}^{n}(\boldsymbol{e}_{q}^{\top}\boldsymbol{z}_{j})A^{(q)}\|_{\rm F}^{2}=\sum_{q=1}^{n}\|A^{(q)}\|_{\rm F}^{2}
so that
with
A^{(q)}=\partial_{lk}[{\mathbb{E}}_{j}\boldsymbol{U}^{\top}\boldsymbol{e}_{q}\boldsymbol{e}_{i}^{\top}{\mathbb{E}}_{j}\boldsymbol{U}]
, the
first factor in the above display is equal to

\displaystyle\quad~\Bigl\{{\mathbb{E}}\sum_{jk,ilq}\Big\|\partial_{lk}\Bigl({\mathbb{E}}_{j}\boldsymbol{U}^{\top}\boldsymbol{e}_{q}\boldsymbol{e}_{i}^{\top}{\mathbb{E}}_{j}\boldsymbol{V}\Bigr)\Big\|_{\rm F}^{2}\Bigl\}^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\text{(i)}}}{{=}}\Bigl\{{\mathbb{E}}\sum_{jk,ilq}\Big\|\partial_{lk}\Bigl({\mathbb{E}}_{j}\boldsymbol{U}^{\top}\Bigr)\boldsymbol{e}_{q}\boldsymbol{e}_{i}^{\top}{\mathbb{E}}_{j}[\boldsymbol{V}]+{\mathbb{E}}_{j}[\boldsymbol{U}]^{\top}\boldsymbol{e}_{q}\boldsymbol{e}_{i}^{\top}\partial_{lk}\Bigl({\mathbb{E}}_{j}[\boldsymbol{V}]\Bigr)\Big\|_{\rm F}^{2}\Bigl\}^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\text{(ii)}}}{{\leq}}\Bigl\{{\mathbb{E}}\sum_{jk,ilq}\Big\|\partial_{lk}\Bigl({\mathbb{E}}_{j}[\boldsymbol{U}^{\top}]\Bigr)\boldsymbol{e}_{q}\boldsymbol{e}_{i}^{\top}{\mathbb{E}}_{j}[\boldsymbol{V}]\Big\|_{\rm F}^{2}\Bigl\}^{\frac{1}{2}}+\Bigl\{{\mathbb{E}}\sum_{jk,ilq}\Big\|{\mathbb{E}}_{j}\boldsymbol{U}^{\top}\boldsymbol{e}_{q}\boldsymbol{e}_{i}^{\top}\partial_{lk}\Bigl({\mathbb{E}}_{j}[\boldsymbol{V}]\Bigr)\Big\|_{\rm F}^{2}\Bigl\}^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\text{(iii)}}}{{=}}\Bigl\{{\mathbb{E}}\sum_{jk,ilq}\Big\|{\mathbb{E}}_{j}[\partial_{lk}\boldsymbol{U}]^{\top}\boldsymbol{e}_{q}\Big\|_{2}^{2}\Big\|\boldsymbol{e}_{i}^{\top}{\mathbb{E}}_{j}\boldsymbol{V}\Big\|_{2}^{2}\Bigl\}^{\frac{1}{2}}+\Bigl\{{\mathbb{E}}\sum_{jk,ilq}\Big\|{\mathbb{E}}_{j}\boldsymbol{U}^{\top}\boldsymbol{e}_{q}\Big\|_{2}^{2}\Big\|\boldsymbol{e}_{i}^{\top}{\mathbb{E}}_{j}[\partial_{lk}\boldsymbol{V}]\Big\|_{2}^{2}\Bigl\}^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\text{(iv)}}}{{=}}\Bigl\{{\mathbb{E}}\sum_{jk,l}\Big\|{\mathbb{E}}_{j}[\partial_{lk}\boldsymbol{U}]^{\top}\Big\|_{\rm F}^{2}\Big\|{\mathbb{E}}_{j}\boldsymbol{V}\Big\|_{\rm F}^{2}\Bigl\}^{\frac{1}{2}}+\Bigl\{{\mathbb{E}}\sum_{jk,l}\Big\|{\mathbb{E}}_{j}\boldsymbol{U}^{\top}\Big\|_{\rm F}^{2}\Big\|{\mathbb{E}}_{j}[\partial_{lk}\boldsymbol{V}]\Big\|_{\rm F}^{2}\Bigl\}^{\frac{1}{2}}

where (i) is the chain rule,
(ii) the triangle inequality,
(iii) holds
provided that the order of the derivation
\partial_{lk}

and the expectation sign
{\mathbb{E}}_{j}
can be switched and using

\|\boldsymbol{a}\boldsymbol{b}^{\top}\|_{\rm F}^{2}=\|\boldsymbol{a}\|_{2}^{2}\|\boldsymbol{b}\|_{2}^{2}
for vectors
\boldsymbol{a},\boldsymbol{b}
,
and (iv) holds using

\sum_{i}\|\boldsymbol{A}\boldsymbol{e}_{i}\|_{2}^{2}=\|\boldsymbol{A}\|_{\rm F}^{2}=\sum_{q}\|\boldsymbol{A}\boldsymbol{e}_{q}\|_{2}^{2}
for a matrix
\boldsymbol{A}
with
n
columns.
Finally, by Jensen’s inequality, the above display is bounded by

\Bigl\{{\mathbb{E}}\sum_{k,l}\Big\|\partial_{lk}\boldsymbol{U}\Big\|_{\rm F}^{2}\sum_{j}\Big\|{\mathbb{E}}_{j}[\boldsymbol{V}]\Big\|_{\rm F}^{2}\Bigl\}^{\frac{1}{2}}+\Bigl\{{\mathbb{E}}\sum_{k,l}\Big\|\partial_{lk}\boldsymbol{V}\Big\|_{\rm F}^{2}\sum_{j}\Big\|{\mathbb{E}}_{j}[\boldsymbol{U}]\Big\|_{\rm F}^{2}\Bigl\}^{\frac{1}{2}}.

Since
\lVert\boldsymbol{U}\rVert_{\rm{F}}\vee\|\boldsymbol{V}\|_{\rm F}\leq 1
almost surely,
the previous display is bounded by

\sqrt{p}(\|\boldsymbol{U}\|_{\partial}+\|\boldsymbol{V}\|_{\partial})
.
In summary,

{\mathbb{E}}[\|\eqref{eq:difficult-term}\|_{\rm F}^{2}]^{\frac{1}{2}}\leq(2p+[2\sqrt{p}(\|\boldsymbol{U}\|_{\partial}+\|\boldsymbol{V}\|_{\partial})]^{2})^{\frac{1}{2}}\leq\sqrt{2p}+2\sqrt{p}(\|\boldsymbol{U}\|_{\partial}+\|\boldsymbol{V}\|_{\partial}).

Combining the bounds on the terms
(
49
)-(
50
)-(
51
)-(
52
)
with the triangle inequality completes the proof.

\blacksquare

Proof of Proposition
F.7
.

Note that
\boldsymbol{Q}_{1}=\frac{\boldsymbol{E}^{\top}\boldsymbol{F}/{\tilde{D}}-\boldsymbol{S}(n\boldsymbol{I}_{T}-\smash{\widehat{\mathbf{A}}})/{\tilde{D}}}{\lVert\boldsymbol{S}^{\frac{1}{2}}\rVert_{\rm{F}}}
, where
{\tilde{D}}=\big(\left\lVert\boldsymbol{F}\right\rVert_{\rm{F}}^{2}+n\trace(\boldsymbol{S})\big)^{\frac{1}{2}}
is defined in
Lemma

F.3
.
Now, Apply
Lemma

F.3
, we obtain

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}\|\boldsymbol{Q}_{1}\|^{2}_{\rm F}={\mathbb{E}}\big[\|\boldsymbol{E}^{\top}\boldsymbol{F}/{\tilde{D}}-\boldsymbol{S}(n\boldsymbol{I}_{T}-\smash{\widehat{\mathbf{A}}})/{\tilde{D}}\|_{\rm F}^{2}\big]\frac{1}{\trace(\boldsymbol{S})}\leq 4.

\blacksquare

Before proving Proposition
F.8
], we state the following
Lemma

H.1
, whose proof is deferred to the end of this section.

Lemma H.1
.

We have

\displaystyle\sum_{ij}\boldsymbol{F}^{\top}\boldsymbol{Z}\boldsymbol{e}_{j}\boldsymbol{e}_{i}^{\top}\frac{\partial\boldsymbol{F}}{\partial z_{ij}}

\displaystyle=\boldsymbol{J}_{1}-\boldsymbol{F}^{\top}\boldsymbol{Z}\boldsymbol{H}(n\boldsymbol{I}_{T}-\smash{\widehat{\mathbf{A}}}),

\displaystyle\sum_{ij}\Big(\frac{\partial\boldsymbol{F}}{\partial z_{ij}}\Big)^{\top}\boldsymbol{Z}\boldsymbol{e}_{j}\boldsymbol{e}_{i}^{\top}\boldsymbol{F}

\displaystyle=\boldsymbol{J}_{2}-\smash{\widehat{\mathbf{A}}}\boldsymbol{F}^{\top}\boldsymbol{F}.

where
\boldsymbol{J}_{1}=\sum_{ij}\boldsymbol{F}^{\top}\boldsymbol{Z}\boldsymbol{e}_{j}\sum_{t}\Delta_{ij}^{it}\boldsymbol{e}_{t}^{\top}
with
\left\lVert\boldsymbol{J}_{1}\right\rVert_{\rm{F}}\leq n^{\frac{1}{2}}\left\lVert\boldsymbol{F}\right\rVert_{\rm{F}}^{2}
, and

\boldsymbol{J}_{2}=\sum_{ijlt}\boldsymbol{e}_{t}D_{ij}^{lt}\boldsymbol{e}_{l}^{\top}\boldsymbol{Z}\boldsymbol{e}_{j}\boldsymbol{e}_{i}^{\top}\boldsymbol{F}
with
\left\lVert\boldsymbol{J}_{2}\right\rVert_{\rm{F}}\leq n^{\frac{1}{2}}\left\lVert\boldsymbol{Z}\right\rVert_{\rm{op}}\lVert\boldsymbol{H}\rVert_{\rm{F}}\left\lVert\boldsymbol{F}\right\rVert_{\rm{F}}
.

Proof of Proposition
F.8
.

We first apply
Lemma

F.4
. To be more specific, let
\boldsymbol{U}=n^{-\frac{1}{2}}\boldsymbol{F}/D
and
\boldsymbol{V}=n^{-\frac{1}{2}}\boldsymbol{Z}^{\top}\boldsymbol{U}
with
D=(\left\lVert\boldsymbol{F}\right\rVert_{\rm{F}}^{2}/n+\lVert\boldsymbol{H}\rVert_{\rm{F}}^{2})^{\frac{1}{2}}
, then
\left\lVert\boldsymbol{U}\right\rVert_{\rm{F}}\leq 1
,
\left\lVert\boldsymbol{V}\right\rVert_{\rm{F}}\leq n^{-\frac{1}{2}}\left\lVert\boldsymbol{Z}\right\rVert_{\rm{op}}
.
Lemma

F.4
yields

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}\Big(\Big\|\boldsymbol{U}^{\top}\boldsymbol{Z}\boldsymbol{V}-\sum_{j=1}^{p}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\frac{\partial}{\partial z_{ij}}\Bigl(\boldsymbol{U}^{\top}\boldsymbol{e}_{i}\boldsymbol{e}_{j}^{\top}\boldsymbol{V}\Bigr)\Big\|_{\rm F}^{2}\Big)

(54)

\displaystyle\leq~

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}[\left\lVert\boldsymbol{U}\right\rVert_{\rm{F}}^{2}\left\lVert\boldsymbol{V}\right\rVert_{\rm{F}}^{2}]+{\mathbb{E}}\sum_{ij}\Big[2\left\lVert\boldsymbol{V}\right\rVert_{\rm{F}}^{2}\left\lVert\frac{\partial\boldsymbol{U}}{\partial z_{ij}}\right\rVert_{\rm{F}}^{2}+2\left\lVert\boldsymbol{U}\right\rVert_{\rm{F}}^{2}\left\lVert\frac{\partial\boldsymbol{V}}{\partial z_{ij}}\right\rVert_{\rm{F}}^{2}\Big]

\displaystyle\leq~

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}\big(n^{-1}\left\lVert\boldsymbol{Z}\right\rVert_{\rm{op}}^{2}\big)+2{\mathbb{E}}\Big(n^{-1}\left\lVert\boldsymbol{Z}\right\rVert_{\rm{op}}^{2}\sum_{ij}\left\lVert\frac{\partial\boldsymbol{U}}{\partial z_{ij}}\right\rVert_{\rm{F}}^{2}\Big)+2{\mathbb{E}}\Big(\sum_{ij}\left\lVert\frac{\partial\boldsymbol{V}}{\partial z_{ij}}\right\rVert_{\rm{F}}^{2}\Big)

(55)

\displaystyle\leq~

\displaystyle\frac{4p}{n}+{\mathbb{E}}\big(\frac{1}{n}\lVert\boldsymbol{Z}\rVert_{\rm{op}}^{2}\big)+6{\mathbb{E}}\Big(n^{-1}\left\lVert\boldsymbol{Z}\right\rVert_{\rm{op}}^{2}\sum_{ij}\left\lVert\frac{\partial\boldsymbol{U}}{\partial z_{ij}}\right\rVert_{\rm{F}}^{2}\Big),

where the last inequality uses the following bound derived using
\boldsymbol{V}=n^{-\frac{1}{2}}\boldsymbol{Z}^{\top}\boldsymbol{U}
, and
\lVert\boldsymbol{U}\rVert_{\rm{F}}\leq 1
,

\displaystyle\sum_{ij}\left\lVert\frac{\partial\boldsymbol{V}}{\partial z_{ij}}\right\rVert_{\rm{F}}^{2}=~

\displaystyle\sum_{ij}n^{-1}\left\lVert\frac{\partial\boldsymbol{Z}^{\top}}{\partial z_{ij}}\boldsymbol{U}+\boldsymbol{Z}^{\top}\frac{\partial\boldsymbol{U}^{\top}}{\partial z_{ij}}\right\rVert_{\rm{F}}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq~

\displaystyle 2{n}^{-1}\Big(p\left\lVert\boldsymbol{U}\right\rVert_{\rm{F}}^{2}+\left\lVert\boldsymbol{Z}\right\rVert_{\rm{op}}^{2}\sum_{ij}\left\lVert\frac{\partial\boldsymbol{U}}{\partial z_{ij}}\right\rVert_{\rm{F}}^{2}\Big)

\displaystyle\leq~

\displaystyle\frac{2p}{n}+2n^{-1}\left\lVert\boldsymbol{Z}\right\rVert_{\rm{op}}^{2}\sum_{ij}\left\lVert\frac{\partial\boldsymbol{U}}{\partial z_{ij}}\right\rVert_{\rm{F}}^{2}.

(56)

Note that
{\mathbb{E}}[(n^{-\frac{1}{2}}\left\lVert\boldsymbol{Z}\right\rVert_{\rm{op}})^{2}]\leq(1+\sqrt{p/n})^{2}+1/n
by (
23
).
Now we establish the connection between
\boldsymbol{Q}_{2}
and the term inside Frobenius norm in (
54
). By definitions of
\boldsymbol{U}
and
\boldsymbol{V}
,

\boldsymbol{U}^{\top}\boldsymbol{Z}\boldsymbol{V}=n^{-\frac{3}{2}}D^{-2}\boldsymbol{F}^{\top}\boldsymbol{Z}\boldsymbol{Z}^{\top}\boldsymbol{F}.

(57)

Next, by product rule,

\displaystyle\sum_{j=1}^{p}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\frac{\partial}{\partial z_{ij}}\Bigl(\boldsymbol{U}^{\top}\boldsymbol{e}_{i}\boldsymbol{e}_{j}^{\top}\boldsymbol{V}\Bigr)

\displaystyle=~

\displaystyle n^{-\frac{3}{2}}\sum_{j=1}^{p}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\frac{\partial}{\partial z_{ij}}\Bigl(\boldsymbol{F}^{\top}\boldsymbol{e}_{i}\boldsymbol{e}_{j}^{\top}\boldsymbol{Z}^{\top}\boldsymbol{F}D^{-2}\Bigr)

\displaystyle=~

\displaystyle n^{-\frac{3}{2}}\sum_{j=1}^{p}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\Big(\underbrace{\frac{\partial\boldsymbol{F}^{\top}}{\partial z_{ij}}\boldsymbol{e}_{i}\boldsymbol{e}_{j}^{\top}\boldsymbol{Z}^{\top}\boldsymbol{F}D^{-2}}_{(i)}+\underbrace{\boldsymbol{F}^{\top}\boldsymbol{e}_{i}\boldsymbol{e}_{j}^{\top}\frac{\partial\boldsymbol{Z}^{\top}\boldsymbol{F}}{\partial z_{ij}}D^{-2}}_{(ii)}+\underbrace{\boldsymbol{F}^{\top}\boldsymbol{e}_{i}\boldsymbol{e}_{j}^{\top}\boldsymbol{Z}^{\top}\boldsymbol{F}\frac{\partial D^{-2}}{\partial z_{ij}}}_{(iii)}\Big).

We now rewrite the above three terms
(i),(ii)
and
(iii)
.

(i)

For term (
i
), by
Lemma

H.1
,

\displaystyle n^{-\frac{3}{2}}D^{-2}\sum_{j=1}^{p}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\frac{\partial\boldsymbol{F}^{\top}}{\partial z_{ij}}\boldsymbol{e}_{i}\boldsymbol{e}_{j}^{\top}\boldsymbol{Z}^{\top}\boldsymbol{F}

\displaystyle=~

\displaystyle n^{-\frac{3}{2}}D^{-2}\bigl[\boldsymbol{J}_{1}-\boldsymbol{F}^{\top}\boldsymbol{Z}\boldsymbol{H}(n\boldsymbol{I}_{T}-\smash{\widehat{\mathbf{A}}})\bigr]^{\top}

\displaystyle=~

\displaystyle n^{-\frac{3}{2}}D^{-2}\bigl[\boldsymbol{J}_{1}^{\top}-(n\boldsymbol{I}_{T}-\smash{\widehat{\mathbf{A}}})\boldsymbol{H}^{\top}\boldsymbol{Z}^{\top}\boldsymbol{F}\bigr].

(ii)

For term (
ii
), by product rule and
Lemma

H.1
,

\displaystyle n^{-\frac{3}{2}}D^{-2}\sum_{j=1}^{p}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\boldsymbol{F}^{\top}\boldsymbol{e}_{i}\boldsymbol{e}_{j}^{\top}\frac{\partial\boldsymbol{Z}^{\top}\boldsymbol{F}}{\partial z_{ij}}

\displaystyle=~

\displaystyle n^{-\frac{3}{2}}D^{-2}\Big(p\boldsymbol{F}^{\top}\boldsymbol{F}+\sum_{j=1}^{p}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\boldsymbol{F}^{\top}\boldsymbol{e}_{i}\boldsymbol{e}_{j}^{\top}\boldsymbol{Z}^{\top}\frac{\partial\boldsymbol{F}}{\partial z_{ij}}\Big)

\displaystyle=~

\displaystyle n^{-\frac{3}{2}}D^{-2}\Big(p\boldsymbol{F}^{\top}\boldsymbol{F}+(\boldsymbol{J}_{2}-\smash{\widehat{\mathbf{A}}}\boldsymbol{F}^{\top}\boldsymbol{F})^{\top}\Big)

\displaystyle=~

\displaystyle n^{-\frac{3}{2}}D^{-2}\big(p\boldsymbol{F}^{\top}\boldsymbol{F}-\boldsymbol{F}^{\top}\boldsymbol{F}\smash{\widehat{\mathbf{A}}}+\boldsymbol{J}_{2}^{\top}\big).

(iii)

For term (
iii
), by chain rule,

\displaystyle n^{-\frac{3}{2}}\sum_{j=1}^{p}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\boldsymbol{F}^{\top}\boldsymbol{e}_{i}\boldsymbol{e}_{j}^{\top}\boldsymbol{Z}^{\top}\boldsymbol{F}\frac{\partial D^{-2}}{\partial z_{ij}}

\displaystyle=~

\displaystyle-2n^{-\frac{3}{2}}D^{-3}\sum_{j=1}^{p}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\boldsymbol{F}^{\top}\boldsymbol{e}_{i}\boldsymbol{e}_{j}^{\top}\boldsymbol{Z}^{\top}\boldsymbol{F}\frac{\partial D}{\partial z_{ij}}

\displaystyle=~

\displaystyle-n^{-\frac{3}{2}}D^{-2}\underbrace{\Big(2D^{-1}\sum_{j=1}^{p}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\boldsymbol{F}^{\top}\boldsymbol{e}_{i}\boldsymbol{e}_{j}^{\top}\boldsymbol{Z}^{\top}\boldsymbol{F}\frac{\partial D}{\partial z_{ij}}\Big)}_{\boldsymbol{J}_{3}}

Combining (
57
) and the above three expressions for (i)-(ii)-(iii),

\displaystyle\boldsymbol{U}^{\top}\boldsymbol{Z}\boldsymbol{V}-\sum_{j=1}^{p}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\frac{\partial}{\partial z_{ij}}\Bigl(\boldsymbol{U}^{\top}\boldsymbol{e}_{i}\boldsymbol{e}_{j}^{\top}\boldsymbol{V}\Bigr)

\displaystyle=~

\displaystyle n^{-\frac{3}{2}}D^{-2}\Bigl[\boldsymbol{F}^{\top}\boldsymbol{Z}\boldsymbol{Z}^{\top}\boldsymbol{F}+(n\boldsymbol{I}_{T}-\smash{\widehat{\mathbf{A}}})\boldsymbol{H}^{\top}\boldsymbol{Z}^{\top}\boldsymbol{F}-p\boldsymbol{F}^{\top}\boldsymbol{F}+\boldsymbol{F}^{\top}\boldsymbol{F}\smash{\widehat{\mathbf{A}}}

\displaystyle-\boldsymbol{J}_{1}^{\top}-\boldsymbol{J}_{2}^{\top}+\boldsymbol{J}_{3}\Bigr]

\displaystyle=~

\displaystyle\boldsymbol{Q}_{2}-n^{-\frac{3}{2}}D^{-2}(\boldsymbol{J}_{1}^{\top}+\boldsymbol{J}_{2}^{\top}-\boldsymbol{J}_{3}).

That is,

\boldsymbol{Q}_{2}=\boldsymbol{U}^{\top}\boldsymbol{Z}\boldsymbol{V}-\sum_{j=1}^{p}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\frac{\partial}{\partial z_{ij}}\Bigl(\boldsymbol{U}^{\top}\boldsymbol{e}_{i}\boldsymbol{e}_{j}^{\top}\boldsymbol{V}\Bigr)+n^{-\frac{3}{2}}D^{-2}(\boldsymbol{J}_{1}^{\top}+\boldsymbol{J}_{2}^{\top}-\boldsymbol{J}_{3}).

(58)

Note that
Lemma

H.1
implies that

\displaystyle n^{-\frac{3}{2}}D^{-2}\left\lVert\boldsymbol{J}_{1}\right\rVert_{\rm{F}}\leq\frac{\left\lVert\boldsymbol{F}\right\rVert_{\rm{F}}^{2}/n}{\left\lVert\boldsymbol{F}\right\rVert_{\rm{F}}^{2}/n+\lVert\boldsymbol{H}\rVert_{\rm{F}}^{2}}\leq 1,

(59)

and

\displaystyle n^{-\frac{3}{2}}D^{-2}\left\lVert\boldsymbol{J}_{2}\right\rVert_{\rm{F}}\leq\Big[n^{-\frac{1}{2}}\left\lVert\boldsymbol{Z}\right\rVert_{\rm{op}}\frac{\left\lVert\boldsymbol{F}\right\rVert_{\rm{F}}n^{-\frac{1}{2}}\lVert\boldsymbol{H}\rVert_{\rm{F}}}{\left\lVert\boldsymbol{F}\right\rVert_{\rm{F}}^{2}/n+\lVert\boldsymbol{H}\rVert_{\rm{F}}^{2}}\Big]\leq\frac{1}{2}(n^{-\frac{1}{2}}\left\lVert\boldsymbol{Z}\right\rVert_{\rm{op}}).

(60)

Since
\boldsymbol{J}_{3}=2D^{-1}\sum_{j=1}^{p}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\boldsymbol{F}^{\top}\boldsymbol{e}_{i}\boldsymbol{e}_{j}^{\top}\boldsymbol{Z}^{\top}\boldsymbol{F}\frac{\partial D}{\partial z_{ij}}
, by Cauchy-Schwarz inequality

\displaystyle n^{-3}D^{-4}\lVert\boldsymbol{J}_{3}\rVert_{\rm{F}}^{2}=

\displaystyle\sum_{t,t^{\prime}}([\boldsymbol{J}_{3}]_{t,t^{\prime}})^{2}

\displaystyle=4n^{-3}D^{-6}\sum_{t,t^{\prime}}\big[\sum_{i,j}\boldsymbol{e}_{t}^{\top}\boldsymbol{F}^{\top}\boldsymbol{e}_{i}\boldsymbol{e}_{j}^{\top}\boldsymbol{Z}^{\top}\boldsymbol{F}\boldsymbol{e}_{t^{\prime}}\frac{\partial D}{\partial z_{ij}}\big]^{2}

\displaystyle\leq 4n^{-3}D^{-6}\sum_{t,t^{\prime}}\Big[\sum_{i,j}[\boldsymbol{e}_{t}^{\top}\boldsymbol{F}^{\top}\boldsymbol{e}_{i}\boldsymbol{e}_{j}^{\top}\boldsymbol{Z}^{\top}\boldsymbol{F}\boldsymbol{e}_{t^{\prime}}]^{2}\sum_{i,j}\big(\frac{\partial D}{\partial z_{ij}}\big)^{2}\Big]

\displaystyle=4n^{-3}D^{-6}\lVert\boldsymbol{F}\rVert_{\rm{F}}^{2}\lVert\boldsymbol{Z}^{\top}\boldsymbol{F}\rVert_{\rm{F}}^{2}\sum_{i,j}\big(\frac{\partial D}{\partial z_{ij}}\big)^{2}

\displaystyle=4n^{-3}D^{-6}\lVert\boldsymbol{F}\rVert_{\rm{F}}^{4}\lVert\boldsymbol{Z}\rVert_{\rm{op}}^{2}\sum_{i,j}\big(\frac{\partial D}{\partial z_{ij}}\big)^{2}.

(1)

Under
4
(i) that
\tau>0
. By
Corollary

D.3
, we have
\sum_{i,j}\big(\frac{\partial D}{\partial z_{ij}}\big)^{2}\leq C(\tau^{\prime})n^{-1}D^{2}
. Then,

n^{-3}D^{-4}\lVert\boldsymbol{J}_{3}\rVert_{\rm{F}}^{2}\leq C(\tau^{\prime})n^{-4}D^{2}\lVert\boldsymbol{F}\rVert_{\rm{F}}^{4}\lVert\boldsymbol{Z}\rVert_{\rm{op}}^{2}\leq C(\tau^{\prime})n^{-2}\lVert\boldsymbol{Z}\rVert_{\rm{op}}^{2},

(61)

by
\left\lVert\boldsymbol{F}\right\rVert_{\rm{F}}^{2}/(nD^{2})\leq 1
.

By (
58
) and triangular inequality, we have

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}[\lVert\boldsymbol{Q}_{2}\rVert_{\rm{F}}^{2}]\leq

\displaystyle 2{\mathbb{E}}\Big[\lVert\boldsymbol{U}^{\top}\boldsymbol{Z}\boldsymbol{V}-\sum_{j=1}^{p}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\frac{\partial}{\partial z_{ij}}\Bigl(\boldsymbol{U}^{\top}\boldsymbol{e}_{i}\boldsymbol{e}_{j}^{\top}\boldsymbol{V}\Bigr)\rVert_{\rm{F}}^{2}\Big]

(62)

\displaystyle+2{\mathbb{E}}[\lVert n^{-\frac{3}{2}}D^{-2}(\boldsymbol{J}_{1}^{\top}+\boldsymbol{J}_{2}^{\top}-\boldsymbol{J}_{3})\rVert_{\rm{F}}^{2}].

By (
59
), (
60
), (
61
), the second term in (
62
) can be upper bounded by

6{\mathbb{E}}[(1+\frac{1}{4}n^{-1}\lVert\boldsymbol{Z}\rVert_{\rm{op}}^{2})+C(\tau^{\prime})n^{-2}\lVert\boldsymbol{Z}\rVert_{\rm{op}}^{2})]\leq C(\tau^{\prime})(1+\frac{p}{n}),

(63)

where the last inequality uses (
23
).

For the first term in (
62
), since
\sum_{ij}\lVert\frac{\partial\boldsymbol{U}}{\partial z_{ij}}\rVert_{\rm{F}}^{2}\leq C(\tau^{\prime})(T\wedge\frac{p}{n})
by
Lemma

D.5
, we have

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}\Big(\Big\|\boldsymbol{U}^{\top}\boldsymbol{Z}\boldsymbol{V}-\sum_{j=1}^{p}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\frac{\partial}{\partial z_{ij}}\Bigl(\boldsymbol{U}^{\top}\boldsymbol{e}_{i}\boldsymbol{e}_{j}^{\top}\boldsymbol{V}\Bigr)\Big\|_{\rm F}^{2}\Big)

\displaystyle\leq~

\displaystyle\frac{4p}{n}+{\mathbb{E}}\big(n^{-1}\lVert\boldsymbol{Z}\rVert_{\rm{op}}^{2}\big)+6{\mathbb{E}}\Big(n^{-1}\lVert\boldsymbol{Z}\rVert_{\rm{op}}^{2}\sum_{ij}\left\lVert\frac{\partial\boldsymbol{U}}{\partial z_{ij}}\right\rVert_{\rm{F}}^{2}\Big)

\displaystyle\leq~

\displaystyle\frac{4p}{n}+[1+C(\tau^{\prime})(T\wedge\frac{p}{n})]{\mathbb{E}}\big(n^{-1}\lVert\boldsymbol{Z}\rVert_{\rm{op}}^{2}\big)

\displaystyle\leq~

\displaystyle\frac{4p}{n}+[1+C(\tau^{\prime})(T\wedge\frac{p}{n})][(1+\sqrt{p/n})^{2}+1/n]

\displaystyle\leq~

\displaystyle C(\tau^{\prime})(T\wedge(1+\frac{p}{n}))(1+\frac{p}{n}).

Therefore, under
4
(i), we obtain

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}[\lVert\boldsymbol{Q}_{2}\rVert_{\rm{F}}^{2}]\leq C(\tau^{\prime})(T\wedge(1+\frac{p}{n}))(1+\frac{p}{n}).

(2)

Under
4
(ii), let
\Omega=U_{1}\cap U_{2}\cap U_{3}
, then we have

{\mathbb{P}}(\Omega^{c})\leq C(\gamma,c)\frac{1}{T}
by (
24
).
By

Lemma

D.2
, on
\Omega
, we have
(i) The map
\boldsymbol{Z}\mapsto\boldsymbol{U}
is
n^{-1/2}L_{1}
-Lipschitz, where
L_{1}=8\max(1,(2\eta)^{-1})
, and
\lVert\boldsymbol{U}\rVert_{\rm{F}}\leq 1
,
(ii) The map
\boldsymbol{Z}\mapsto\boldsymbol{V}
is
n^{-1/2}L_{2}
-Lipschitz, where
L_{2}=(1+(2+\sqrt{p/n})L_{1})
, and
\lVert\boldsymbol{V}\rVert_{\rm{F}}\leq(2+\sqrt{p/n})
.
Applying
Corollary

F.5
with
K=2+\sqrt{p/n}
yields

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}\Big[I(\Omega)\lVert\boldsymbol{U}^{\top}\boldsymbol{Z}\boldsymbol{V}-\sum_{j=1}^{p}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\frac{\partial}{\partial z_{ij}}\Bigl(\boldsymbol{U}^{\top}\boldsymbol{e}_{i}\boldsymbol{e}_{j}^{\top}\boldsymbol{V}\Bigr)\rVert_{\rm{F}}^{2}\Big]

\displaystyle\leq~

\displaystyle K^{2}+C(\gamma,c)(K^{2}L_{1}^{2}+L_{2}^{2})+2{\mathbb{E}}\Big[I(\Omega)\sum_{ij}\Big(K^{2}\left\lVert\frac{\partial\boldsymbol{U}}{\partial z_{ij}}\right\rVert_{\rm{F}}^{2}+\left\lVert\frac{\partial\boldsymbol{V}}{\partial z_{ij}}\right\rVert_{\rm{F}}^{2}\Big)\Big]

\displaystyle\leq~

\displaystyle C(\gamma,c),

where the last inequality holds because
K\leq C(\gamma),L_{1}=C(\gamma,c),L_{2}=C(\gamma,c)
, and on
\Omega
,
\sum_{ij}\lVert\frac{\partial\boldsymbol{U}}{\partial z_{ij}}\rVert_{\rm{F}}^{2}\leq C(\gamma,c)
from
Lemma

D.5
, and
{\mathbb{E}}[\lVert\frac{\partial\boldsymbol{V}}{\partial z_{ij}}\rVert_{\rm{F}}^{2}]\leq C(\gamma,c)
by product rule. Therefore, under
4
(i), we obtain

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}[I(\Omega)\lVert\boldsymbol{Q}_{2}\rVert_{\rm{F}}^{2}]\leq~

\displaystyle 2{\mathbb{E}}\Big[\lVert\boldsymbol{U}^{\top}\boldsymbol{Z}\boldsymbol{V}-\sum_{j=1}^{p}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\frac{\partial}{\partial z_{ij}}\Bigl(\boldsymbol{U}^{\top}\boldsymbol{e}_{i}\boldsymbol{e}_{j}^{\top}\boldsymbol{V}\Bigr)\rVert_{\rm{F}}^{2}\Big]

\displaystyle+2{\mathbb{E}}[\lVert n^{-\frac{3}{2}}D^{-2}(\boldsymbol{J}_{1}^{\top}+\boldsymbol{J}_{2}^{\top}-\boldsymbol{J}_{3})\rVert_{\rm{F}}^{2}]

\displaystyle\leq~

\displaystyle C(\gamma,c),

where the last inequality used (
59
), (
60
), and that

n^{-3}{\mathbb{E}}[D^{-4}\lVert\boldsymbol{J}_{3}\rVert_{\rm{F}}^{2}]\leq C(\gamma,c)
in analogy to (
61
).

\blacksquare

Proof of Proposition
F.9
.

We will apply
Lemma

F.6
.
Let
\boldsymbol{U}=\boldsymbol{V}=n^{-\frac{1}{2}}D^{-1}\boldsymbol{F}
with
D=(\left\lVert\boldsymbol{F}\right\rVert_{\rm{F}}^{2}/n+\lVert\boldsymbol{H}\rVert_{\rm{F}}^{2})^{\frac{1}{2}}
, then
\left\lVert\boldsymbol{U}\right\rVert_{\rm{F}}=\left\lVert\boldsymbol{V}\right\rVert_{\rm{F}}\leq 1
. Let
\boldsymbol{W}_{0}=p\boldsymbol{U}^{\top}\boldsymbol{U}-\sum_{j=1}^{p}\big(\sum_{i=1}^{n}\frac{\partial\boldsymbol{U}^{\top}\boldsymbol{e}_{i}}{\partial z_{ij}}-\boldsymbol{U}^{\top}\boldsymbol{Z}\boldsymbol{e}_{j}\big)\big(\sum_{i=1}^{n}\frac{\partial\boldsymbol{U}^{\top}\boldsymbol{e}_{i}}{\partial z_{ij}}-\boldsymbol{U}^{\top}\boldsymbol{Z}\boldsymbol{e}_{j}\big)^{\top}
,
then
Lemma

F.6
gives

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}[\left\lVert\boldsymbol{W}_{0}\right\rVert_{\rm{F}}]

\displaystyle\leq 2\|\boldsymbol{U}\|_{\partial}\|\boldsymbol{V}\|_{\partial}+\sqrt{p}\bigl(\sqrt{2}+(3+\sqrt{2})(\|\boldsymbol{U}\|_{\partial}+\|\boldsymbol{V}\|_{\partial})\bigr)

\displaystyle=2\|\boldsymbol{U}\|_{\partial}^{2}+\sqrt{p}(\sqrt{2}+2(3+\sqrt{2})\|\boldsymbol{U}\|_{\partial}).

We will prove under
4
(i), and the proof under
4
(ii) on set
\Omega=U_{1}\cap U_{2}\cap U_{3}
follows from almost similar arguments with
\tau^{\prime}
replaced by
\eta
, which is a constant that depends only on
\gamma,c
.

By definition of
\left\lVert\boldsymbol{U}\right\rVert_{\partial}
and
Lemma

D.5
,

\lVert\boldsymbol{U}\rVert_{\partial}^{2}=\sum_{ij}{\mathbb{E}}\lVert\frac{\partial\boldsymbol{U}}{\partial z_{ij}}\rVert_{\rm{F}}^{2}\leq C(\gamma,\tau^{\prime})
. Thus
{\mathbb{E}}[\left\lVert\boldsymbol{W}_{0}\right\rVert_{\rm{F}}]\leq C(\tau^{\prime},\gamma)\sqrt{p}
.

Now we establish the connection between
\boldsymbol{W}_{0}
and
\boldsymbol{Q}_{3}
. Since
\boldsymbol{U}=n^{-\frac{1}{2}}D^{-1}\boldsymbol{F}
, by product rule,

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{n}\frac{\partial\boldsymbol{U}^{\top}\boldsymbol{e}_{i}}{\partial z_{ij}}-\boldsymbol{U}^{\top}\boldsymbol{Z}\boldsymbol{e}_{j}

\displaystyle=~

\displaystyle n^{-\frac{1}{2}}\Big(\sum_{i=1}^{n}\frac{\partial D^{-1}\boldsymbol{F}^{\top}\boldsymbol{e}_{i}}{\partial z_{ij}}-D^{-1}\boldsymbol{F}^{\top}\boldsymbol{Z}\boldsymbol{e}_{j}\Big)

\displaystyle=~

\displaystyle n^{-\frac{1}{2}}\Big(\sum_{i=1}^{n}D^{-1}\frac{\partial\boldsymbol{F}^{\top}\boldsymbol{e}_{i}}{\partial z_{ij}}-D^{-1}\boldsymbol{F}^{\top}\boldsymbol{Z}\boldsymbol{e}_{j}\Big)+n^{-\frac{1}{2}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\boldsymbol{F}^{\top}\boldsymbol{e}_{i}\frac{\partial D^{-1}}{\partial z_{ij}}

\displaystyle=~

\displaystyle n^{-\frac{1}{2}}D^{-1}\Big(\sum_{i=1}^{n}\frac{\partial\boldsymbol{F}^{\top}\boldsymbol{e}_{i}}{\partial z_{ij}}-\boldsymbol{F}^{\top}\boldsymbol{Z}\boldsymbol{e}_{j}\Big)-n^{-\frac{1}{2}}D^{-2}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\boldsymbol{F}^{\top}\boldsymbol{e}_{i}\frac{\partial D}{\partial z_{ij}}.

For the first term in the last display, we have by
Lemma

D.4

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{n}\frac{\partial\boldsymbol{e}_{i}^{\top}\boldsymbol{F}}{\partial z_{ij}}=\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\frac{\partial\boldsymbol{e}_{i}^{\top}\boldsymbol{F}\boldsymbol{e}_{t}}{\partial z_{ij}}\boldsymbol{e}_{t}^{\top}=\sum_{it}(D_{ij}^{it}+\Delta_{ij}^{it})\boldsymbol{e}_{t}^{\top}=-\boldsymbol{e}_{j}^{\top}\boldsymbol{H}(n\boldsymbol{I}_{T}-\smash{\widehat{\mathbf{A}}})+\sum_{it}\Delta_{ij}^{it}\boldsymbol{e}_{t}^{\top}.

Hence,

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{n}\frac{\partial\boldsymbol{U}^{\top}\boldsymbol{e}_{i}}{\partial z_{ij}}-\boldsymbol{U}^{\top}\boldsymbol{Z}\boldsymbol{e}_{j}

\displaystyle=~

\displaystyle n^{-\frac{1}{2}}D^{-1}\big[-(n\boldsymbol{I}_{T}-\smash{\widehat{\mathbf{A}}})\boldsymbol{H}^{\top}\boldsymbol{e}_{j}+\sum_{it}\Delta_{ij}^{it}\boldsymbol{e}_{t}-\boldsymbol{F}^{\top}\boldsymbol{Z}\boldsymbol{e}_{j}\big]-n^{-\frac{1}{2}}D^{-2}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\boldsymbol{F}^{\top}\boldsymbol{e}_{i}\frac{\partial D}{\partial z_{ij}}

\displaystyle=~

\displaystyle-n^{-\frac{1}{2}}D^{-1}\big[(n\boldsymbol{I}_{T}-\smash{\widehat{\mathbf{A}}})\boldsymbol{H}^{\top}+\boldsymbol{F}^{\top}\boldsymbol{Z}\big]\boldsymbol{e}_{j}+n^{-\frac{1}{2}}D^{-1}\sum_{it}\Delta_{ij}^{it}\boldsymbol{e}_{t}-n^{-\frac{1}{2}}D^{-2}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\boldsymbol{F}^{\top}\boldsymbol{e}_{i}\frac{\partial D}{\partial z_{ij}}.

(64)

Let
\boldsymbol{W}_{1}=-n^{-\frac{1}{2}}D^{-1}\big[(n\boldsymbol{I}_{T}-\smash{\widehat{\mathbf{A}}})\boldsymbol{H}^{\top}+\boldsymbol{F}^{\top}\boldsymbol{Z}\big]
be the first term in (
64
).
For the second term in (
64
), recall

\Delta_{ij}^{lt}=-(\boldsymbol{e}_{t}^{\top}\otimes\boldsymbol{e}_{l}^{\top})(\boldsymbol{I}_{T}\otimes\boldsymbol{X})\boldsymbol{M}^{\dagger}(\boldsymbol{I}_{T}\otimes\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{\frac{1}{2}})\bigl(\boldsymbol{F}^{\top}\otimes\boldsymbol{I}_{p}\bigr)(\boldsymbol{e}_{i}\otimes\boldsymbol{e}_{j})
in
Lemma

D.4
,

\displaystyle n^{-\frac{1}{2}}D^{-1}\sum_{it}\Delta_{ij}^{it}\boldsymbol{e}_{t}

\displaystyle=~

\displaystyle-n^{-\frac{1}{2}}D^{-1}\sum_{it}\boldsymbol{e}_{t}(\boldsymbol{e}_{t}^{\top}\otimes\boldsymbol{e}_{i}^{\top})(\boldsymbol{I}_{T}\otimes\boldsymbol{X})\boldsymbol{M}^{\dagger}(\boldsymbol{I}_{T}\otimes\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{\frac{1}{2}})\bigl(\boldsymbol{F}^{\top}\boldsymbol{e}_{i}\otimes\boldsymbol{e}_{j}\bigr)

\displaystyle=~

\displaystyle-n^{-\frac{1}{2}}D^{-1}\sum_{i}(\boldsymbol{I}_{T}\otimes\boldsymbol{e}_{i}^{\top}\boldsymbol{X})\boldsymbol{M}^{\dagger}(\boldsymbol{I}_{T}\otimes\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{\frac{1}{2}})\bigl(\boldsymbol{F}^{\top}\boldsymbol{e}_{i}\otimes\boldsymbol{I}_{p}\bigr)\boldsymbol{e}_{j}

\displaystyle=~

\displaystyle-n^{-\frac{1}{2}}D^{-1}\sum_{i=1}^{n}(\boldsymbol{I}_{T}\otimes\boldsymbol{e}_{i}^{\top}\boldsymbol{X})\boldsymbol{M}^{\dagger}(\boldsymbol{I}_{T}\otimes\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{\frac{1}{2}})(\boldsymbol{F}^{\top}\boldsymbol{e}_{i}\otimes\boldsymbol{I}_{p})\boldsymbol{e}_{j}

\displaystyle=~

\displaystyle\boldsymbol{W}_{2}\boldsymbol{e}_{j},

where
\boldsymbol{W}_{2}=-n^{-\frac{1}{2}}D^{-1}\sum_{i=1}^{n}(\boldsymbol{I}_{T}\otimes\boldsymbol{e}_{i}^{\top}\boldsymbol{X})\boldsymbol{M}^{\dagger}(\boldsymbol{I}_{T}\otimes\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{\frac{1}{2}})(\boldsymbol{F}^{\top}\boldsymbol{e}_{i}\otimes\boldsymbol{I}_{p})
.
For the third term in (
64
),

-n^{-\frac{1}{2}}D^{-2}\boldsymbol{F}^{\top}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\boldsymbol{e}_{i}\frac{\partial D}{\partial z_{ij}}=\boldsymbol{W}_{3}\boldsymbol{e}_{j},

where
\boldsymbol{W}_{3}=-n^{-\frac{1}{2}}D^{-2}\boldsymbol{F}^{\top}\frac{\partial D}{\partial\boldsymbol{Z}}
, here we slightly abuse the notation and let
\frac{\partial D}{\partial\boldsymbol{Z}}
denote the
n\times p
matrix with
(i,j)
-th entry being
\frac{\partial D}{\partial z_{ij}}
.
Therefore, (
64
) can be simplified as

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{n}\frac{\partial\boldsymbol{U}^{\top}\boldsymbol{e}_{i}}{\partial z_{ij}}-\boldsymbol{U}^{\top}\boldsymbol{Z}\boldsymbol{e}_{j}=\big[\boldsymbol{W}_{1}+\boldsymbol{W}_{2}+\boldsymbol{W}_{3}\big]\boldsymbol{e}_{j}.

Furthermore,

\displaystyle\boldsymbol{W}_{0}

\displaystyle=p\boldsymbol{U}^{\top}\boldsymbol{U}-\sum_{j=1}^{p}\Big(\sum_{i=1}^{n}\frac{\partial\boldsymbol{U}^{\top}\boldsymbol{e}_{i}}{\partial z_{ij}}-\boldsymbol{U}^{\top}\boldsymbol{Z}\boldsymbol{e}_{j}\Big)\Big(\sum_{i=1}^{n}\frac{\partial\boldsymbol{U}^{\top}\boldsymbol{e}_{i}}{\partial z_{ij}}-\boldsymbol{U}^{\top}\boldsymbol{Z}\boldsymbol{e}_{j}\Big)^{\top}

\displaystyle=p\boldsymbol{U}^{\top}\boldsymbol{U}-[\boldsymbol{W}_{1}+\boldsymbol{W}_{2}+\boldsymbol{W}_{3}][\boldsymbol{W}_{1}+\boldsymbol{W}_{2}+\boldsymbol{W}_{3}]^{\top}

\displaystyle=n^{\frac{1}{2}}\boldsymbol{Q}_{3}-\boldsymbol{W}_{1}(\boldsymbol{W}_{2}+\boldsymbol{W}_{3})^{\top}-(\boldsymbol{W}_{2}+\boldsymbol{W}_{3})\boldsymbol{W}_{1}^{\top}-(\boldsymbol{W}_{2}+\boldsymbol{W}_{3})(\boldsymbol{W}_{2}+\boldsymbol{W}_{3})^{\top},

where the last equality is due to

\displaystyle p\boldsymbol{U}^{\top}\boldsymbol{U}-\boldsymbol{W}_{1}\boldsymbol{W}_{1}^{\top}

\displaystyle=~

\displaystyle n^{-1}D^{-2}\Big[p\boldsymbol{F}^{\top}\boldsymbol{F}-\boldsymbol{F}^{\top}\boldsymbol{Z}\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{-1}\boldsymbol{Z}^{\top}\boldsymbol{F}-(n\boldsymbol{I}_{T}-\smash{\widehat{\mathbf{A}}})\boldsymbol{H}^{\top}\boldsymbol{H}(n\boldsymbol{I}_{T}-\smash{\widehat{\mathbf{A}}})

\displaystyle-(n\boldsymbol{I}_{T}-\smash{\widehat{\mathbf{A}}})\boldsymbol{H}^{\top}\boldsymbol{Z}^{\top}\boldsymbol{F}-\boldsymbol{F}^{\top}\boldsymbol{Z}\boldsymbol{H}(n\boldsymbol{I}_{T}-\smash{\widehat{\mathbf{A}}})\Big]

\displaystyle=~

\displaystyle n^{\frac{1}{2}}\boldsymbol{Q}_{3}.

Therefore,

\displaystyle\boldsymbol{Q}_{3}=n^{-\frac{1}{2}}\Big[\boldsymbol{W}_{0}+\boldsymbol{W}_{1}(\boldsymbol{W}_{2}+\boldsymbol{W}_{3})^{\top}+(\boldsymbol{W}_{2}+\boldsymbol{W}_{3})\boldsymbol{W}_{1}^{\top}+(\boldsymbol{W}_{2}+\boldsymbol{W}_{3})(\boldsymbol{W}_{2}+\boldsymbol{W}_{3})^{\top}\Big].

(65)

We then bound the norms of
\boldsymbol{W}_{1},\boldsymbol{W}_{2},\boldsymbol{W}_{3}
. For
\boldsymbol{W}_{1}
,

\displaystyle\left\lVert\boldsymbol{W}_{1}\right\rVert_{\rm{F}}

\displaystyle=n^{-\frac{1}{2}}D^{-1}\lVert(n\boldsymbol{I}_{T}-\smash{\widehat{\mathbf{A}}})\boldsymbol{H}^{\top}+\boldsymbol{F}^{\top}\boldsymbol{Z}\rVert_{\rm{F}}

\displaystyle\leq n^{\frac{1}{2}}\big(D^{-1}\lVert\boldsymbol{H}\rVert_{\rm{F}}+n^{-1}D^{-1}\left\lVert\boldsymbol{F}\right\rVert_{\rm{F}}\left\lVert\boldsymbol{Z}\right\rVert_{\rm{op}}\big)

\displaystyle\leq n^{\frac{1}{2}}+n^{\frac{1}{2}}\big(D^{-1}\left\lVert\boldsymbol{F}\right\rVert_{\rm{F}}/\sqrt{n}\left\lVert\boldsymbol{Z}/\sqrt{n}\right\rVert_{\rm{op}}\big)

\displaystyle\leq n^{\frac{1}{2}}(1+\lVert\boldsymbol{Z}/\sqrt{n}\rVert_{\rm{op}}),

where we used
\left\lVert\boldsymbol{I}_{T}-\smash{\widehat{\mathbf{A}}}/n\right\rVert_{\rm{op}}\leq 1
by
Lemma

C.1
,
D^{-1}\lVert\boldsymbol{H}\rVert_{\rm{F}}\leq 1
, and
D^{-1}\left\lVert\boldsymbol{F}\right\rVert_{\rm{F}}/\sqrt{n}\leq 1
.

For
\boldsymbol{W}_{2}
,

\displaystyle\left\lVert\boldsymbol{W}_{2}\right\rVert_{\rm{op}}

\displaystyle=n^{-\frac{1}{2}}D^{-1}\Big\lVert\sum_{i=1}^{n}(\boldsymbol{I}_{T}\otimes\boldsymbol{e}_{i}^{\top}\boldsymbol{X})\boldsymbol{M}^{\dagger}(\boldsymbol{I}_{T}\otimes\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{\frac{1}{2}})(\boldsymbol{F}^{\top}\boldsymbol{e}_{i}\otimes\boldsymbol{I}_{p})\Big\rVert_{\rm{op}}

\displaystyle\leq n^{-\frac{1}{2}}D^{-1}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\lVert(\boldsymbol{I}_{T}\otimes\boldsymbol{e}_{i}^{\top}\boldsymbol{X})\boldsymbol{M}^{\dagger}(\boldsymbol{I}_{T}\otimes\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{\frac{1}{2}})(\boldsymbol{F}^{\top}\boldsymbol{e}_{i}\otimes\boldsymbol{I}_{p})\rVert_{\rm{op}}

\displaystyle\leq n^{-\frac{1}{2}}D^{-1}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\lVert(\boldsymbol{I}_{T}\otimes\boldsymbol{e}_{i}^{\top}\boldsymbol{X})\boldsymbol{M}^{\dagger}(\boldsymbol{I}_{T}\otimes\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{\frac{1}{2}})\rVert_{\rm{op}}\lVert(\boldsymbol{F}^{\top}\boldsymbol{e}_{i}\otimes\boldsymbol{I}_{p})\rVert_{\rm{op}}

\displaystyle\leq n^{-\frac{1}{2}}\lVert\boldsymbol{\Sigma}\rVert_{\rm{op}}^{\frac{1}{2}}D^{-1}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\lVert(\boldsymbol{I}_{T}\otimes\boldsymbol{e}_{i}^{\top}\boldsymbol{X})(\boldsymbol{I}_{T}\otimes(\boldsymbol{X}_{\hat{{S}}}^{\top}\boldsymbol{X}_{\hat{{S}}}+n\tau\boldsymbol{P}_{\hat{{S}}})^{\dagger})\rVert_{\rm{op}}\lVert(\boldsymbol{F}^{\top}\boldsymbol{e}_{i}\otimes\boldsymbol{I}_{p})\rVert_{\rm{op}}

\displaystyle=n^{-\frac{1}{2}}\lVert\boldsymbol{\Sigma}\rVert_{\rm{op}}^{\frac{1}{2}}D^{-1}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\lVert\boldsymbol{I}_{T}\otimes[\boldsymbol{e}_{i}^{\top}\boldsymbol{X}_{\hat{{S}}}(\boldsymbol{X}_{\hat{{S}}}^{\top}\boldsymbol{X}_{\hat{{S}}}+n\tau\boldsymbol{P}_{\hat{{S}}})^{\dagger}]\rVert_{\rm{op}}\lVert\boldsymbol{F}^{\top}\boldsymbol{e}_{i}\rVert

\displaystyle\leq n^{-\frac{1}{2}}\lVert\boldsymbol{\Sigma}\rVert_{\rm{op}}^{\frac{1}{2}}D^{-1}\lVert\boldsymbol{X}_{\hat{{S}}}(\boldsymbol{X}_{\hat{{S}}}^{\top}\boldsymbol{X}_{\hat{{S}}}+n\tau\boldsymbol{P}_{\hat{{S}}})^{\dagger}\rVert_{\rm{op}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\lVert\boldsymbol{F}^{\top}\boldsymbol{e}_{i}\rVert

\displaystyle\leq n^{-\frac{1}{2}}D^{-1}n^{-\frac{1}{2}}(\tau/\lVert\boldsymbol{\Sigma}\rVert_{\rm{op}})^{-\frac{1}{2}}n^{\frac{1}{2}}\lVert\boldsymbol{F}\rVert_{\rm{F}}

\displaystyle\leq(\tau^{\prime})^{-\frac{1}{2}},

where the third inequality uses
\boldsymbol{M}^{\dagger}\preceq\boldsymbol{I}_{T}\otimes(\boldsymbol{X}_{\hat{{S}}}^{\top}\boldsymbol{X}_{\hat{{S}}}+n\tau\boldsymbol{P}_{\hat{{S}}})^{\dagger}
,
the fourth inequality uses the result that
\lVert\boldsymbol{e}_{i}^{\top}\boldsymbol{A}\rVert_{\rm{op}}\leq\lVert\boldsymbol{A}\rVert_{\rm{op}}
,
the penultimate inequality uses
\lVert\boldsymbol{X}_{\hat{{S}}}(\boldsymbol{X}_{\hat{{S}}}^{\top}\boldsymbol{X}_{\hat{{S}}}+n\tau\boldsymbol{P}_{\hat{{S}}})^{\dagger}\rVert_{\rm{op}}\leq(n\tau)^{-1/2}
and the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality,
the last inequality follows from
n^{-1/2}D^{-1}\lVert\boldsymbol{F}\rVert_{\rm{F}}\leq 1
.
It immediately follows that
\lVert\boldsymbol{W}_{2}\rVert_{\rm{F}}\leq\sqrt{T}C(\tau^{\prime})
since the rank of
\boldsymbol{W}_{2}
is at most
T
.

For
\boldsymbol{W}_{3}
, using
n^{-\frac{1}{2}}D^{-1}\left\lVert\boldsymbol{F}\right\rVert_{\rm{F}}\leq 1
, and
\left\lVert\frac{\partial D}{\partial\boldsymbol{Z}}\right\rVert_{\rm{F}}\leq n^{-\frac{1}{2}}DC(\tau^{\prime})
from
Corollary

D.3
, we obtain

\displaystyle\left\lVert\boldsymbol{W}_{3}\right\rVert_{\rm{F}}=n^{-\frac{1}{2}}D^{-2}\lVert\boldsymbol{F}^{\top}\frac{\partial D}{\partial\boldsymbol{Z}}\rVert_{\rm{F}}\leq n^{-\frac{1}{2}}D^{-2}\left\lVert\boldsymbol{F}\right\rVert_{\rm{F}}\left\lVert\frac{\partial D}{\partial\boldsymbol{Z}}\right\rVert_{\rm{F}}\leq D^{-1}\left\lVert\frac{\partial D}{\partial\boldsymbol{Z}}\right\rVert_{\rm{F}}\leq n^{-1/2}C(\tau^{\prime}),

The desired inequality follows by combining (
65
) and the bounds for
\boldsymbol{W}_{0},\boldsymbol{W}_{1},\boldsymbol{W}_{2},\boldsymbol{W}_{3}
.

\blacksquare

Proof of
Lemma

H.1
.

By
Lemma

D.4
,
\frac{\partial F_{lt}}{\partial z_{ij}}=D_{ij}^{lt}+\Delta_{ij}^{lt}
, where

\displaystyle D_{ij}^{lt}

\displaystyle=-(\boldsymbol{e}_{j}^{\top}\boldsymbol{H}\otimes\boldsymbol{e}_{i}^{\top})(\boldsymbol{I}_{nT}-\boldsymbol{N})(\boldsymbol{e}_{t}\otimes\boldsymbol{e}_{l}),

(66)

\displaystyle\Delta_{ij}^{lt}

\displaystyle=-(\boldsymbol{e}_{t}^{\top}\otimes\boldsymbol{e}_{l}^{\top})(\boldsymbol{I}_{T}\otimes\boldsymbol{X})\boldsymbol{M}^{\dagger}(\boldsymbol{I}_{T}\otimes\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{\frac{1}{2}})\bigl(\boldsymbol{F}^{\top}\otimes\boldsymbol{I}_{p}\bigr)(\boldsymbol{e}_{i}\otimes\boldsymbol{e}_{j}).

(67)

For the first equality, since
\boldsymbol{e}_{i}^{\top}\frac{\partial\boldsymbol{F}}{\partial z_{ij}}=\sum_{t}(D_{ij}^{it}+\Delta_{ij}^{it})\boldsymbol{e}_{t}^{\top}
, we have

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{j=1}^{p}\boldsymbol{F}^{\top}\boldsymbol{Z}\boldsymbol{e}_{j}\boldsymbol{e}_{i}^{\top}\frac{\partial\boldsymbol{F}}{\partial z_{ij}}

\displaystyle=~

\displaystyle\sum_{ij}\boldsymbol{F}^{\top}\boldsymbol{Z}\boldsymbol{e}_{j}\sum_{t=1}^{T}(D_{ij}^{it}+\Delta_{ij}^{it})\boldsymbol{e}_{t}^{\top}

\displaystyle=~

\displaystyle\sum_{ij}\boldsymbol{F}^{\top}\boldsymbol{Z}\boldsymbol{e}_{j}\sum_{t}D_{ij}^{it}\boldsymbol{e}_{t}^{\top}+\underbrace{\sum_{ij}\boldsymbol{F}^{\top}\boldsymbol{Z}\boldsymbol{e}_{j}\sum_{t}p\Delta_{ij}^{it}\boldsymbol{e}_{t}^{\top}}_{\boldsymbol{J}_{1}},

where the first term can be simplified as below

\displaystyle\sum_{j=1}^{p}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\boldsymbol{F}^{\top}\boldsymbol{Z}\boldsymbol{e}_{j}\sum_{t=1}^{T}D_{ij}^{it}\boldsymbol{e}_{t}^{\top}

\displaystyle=~

\displaystyle-\sum_{j=1}^{p}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\boldsymbol{F}^{\top}\boldsymbol{Z}\boldsymbol{e}_{j}\sum_{t=1}^{T}(\boldsymbol{e}_{j}^{\top}\boldsymbol{H}\otimes\boldsymbol{e}_{i}^{\top})(\boldsymbol{I}_{nT}-\boldsymbol{N})(\boldsymbol{e}_{t}\otimes\boldsymbol{e}_{i})\boldsymbol{e}_{t}^{\top}

\displaystyle=~

\displaystyle-\boldsymbol{F}^{\top}\boldsymbol{Z}\boldsymbol{H}\Bigl[\sum_{i}(\boldsymbol{I}_{T}\otimes\boldsymbol{e}_{i}^{\top})(\boldsymbol{I}_{nT}-\boldsymbol{N})(\boldsymbol{I}_{T}\otimes\boldsymbol{e}_{i})\Bigr]

\displaystyle=~

\displaystyle-\boldsymbol{F}^{\top}\boldsymbol{Z}\boldsymbol{H}(n\boldsymbol{I}_{T}-\smash{\widehat{\mathbf{A}}}).

For the second equality, since
\frac{\partial\boldsymbol{F}}{\partial z_{ij}}=\sum_{lt}\boldsymbol{e}_{l}(D_{ij}^{lt}+\Delta_{ij}^{lt})\boldsymbol{e}_{t}^{\top}
,

\displaystyle\sum_{ij}\Big(\frac{\partial\boldsymbol{F}}{\partial z_{ij}}\Big)^{\top}\boldsymbol{Z}\boldsymbol{e}_{j}\boldsymbol{e}_{i}^{\top}\boldsymbol{F}=\underbrace{\sum_{ijtl}\boldsymbol{e}_{t}D_{ij}^{lt}\boldsymbol{e}_{l}^{\top}\boldsymbol{Z}\boldsymbol{e}_{j}\boldsymbol{e}_{i}^{\top}\boldsymbol{F}}_{\boldsymbol{J}_{2}}+\sum_{ijtl}\boldsymbol{e}_{t}\Delta_{ij}^{lt}\boldsymbol{e}_{l}^{\top}\boldsymbol{Z}\boldsymbol{e}_{j}\boldsymbol{e}_{i}^{\top}\boldsymbol{F},

where the second term can be simplified as below,

\displaystyle\sum_{ijtl}\boldsymbol{e}_{t}\Delta_{ij}^{lt}\boldsymbol{e}_{l}^{\top}\boldsymbol{Z}\boldsymbol{e}_{j}\boldsymbol{e}_{i}^{\top}\boldsymbol{F}

\displaystyle~=

\displaystyle\sum_{ijtl}\boldsymbol{e}_{t}\Delta_{ij}^{lt}(\boldsymbol{e}_{i}\otimes\boldsymbol{e}_{j})^{\top}(\boldsymbol{F}\otimes\boldsymbol{Z}^{\top}\boldsymbol{e}_{l})

\displaystyle~=

\displaystyle-\sum_{ijtl}\boldsymbol{e}_{t}(\boldsymbol{e}_{t}^{\top}\otimes\boldsymbol{e}_{l}^{\top})(\boldsymbol{I}_{T}\otimes\boldsymbol{X})\boldsymbol{M}^{\dagger}(\boldsymbol{I}_{T}\otimes\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{\frac{1}{2}})\bigl(\boldsymbol{F}^{\top}\otimes\boldsymbol{I}_{p}\bigr)(\boldsymbol{e}_{i}\otimes\boldsymbol{e}_{j})(\boldsymbol{e}_{i}\otimes\boldsymbol{e}_{j})^{\top}(\boldsymbol{F}\otimes\boldsymbol{Z}^{\top}\boldsymbol{e}_{l})

\displaystyle~=
